to Proposition
2.26
describing adjoints of boundary conditions defined from projectors.

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
. We assume that
P_{\mathcal{C}}\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
is a Calderón projection for
D
and
P_{\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}}\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
is a Calderón projection for
D^{\dagger}
. Then

a

extends to a continuous and invertible operator

\scalebox{1.5}{a}:\begin{matrix}(1-P_{\mathcal{C}}){\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\\
\bigoplus\\
P_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\end{matrix}\longrightarrow\begin{matrix}(1-P_{\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}}^{*}){\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}}(\Sigma;F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\\
\bigoplus\\
P_{\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}}^{*}{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\end{matrix}.

Moreover, in this decomposition

\scalebox{1.5}{a}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\scalebox{1.5}{a}^{+}\\
\scalebox{1.5}{a}^{-}&0\end{pmatrix}+\Psi^{-\infty}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m},F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),

where

\scalebox{1.5}{a}^{+}=(1-P_{\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}}^{*})\scalebox{1.5}{a}P_{\mathcal{C}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\scalebox{1.5}{a}^{-}=P_{\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}}^{*}\scalebox{1.5}{a}(1-P_{\mathcal{C}}).

Proof.

This follows directly from Proposition
4.4
and the relation between
P_{\mathcal{C}}
and
P_{\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}}
from Theorem
4.6
.
∎

Definition 4.9
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
. We define the space

\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\begin{matrix}(1-P_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}){\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\\
\bigoplus\\
P_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\end{matrix}

Lemma 4.10
.

The
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing induces a perfect pairing
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)\times\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)^{\ast}
and hence a Banach space isomorphism

\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)^{*}.

Moreover, the operator

a

defines an isomorphism

\scalebox{1.5}{a}:\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)\to\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger}).

Proof.

By Theorem
3.18
we have that

\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\begin{matrix}(1-P_{\mathcal{C}}){\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\\
\bigoplus\\
P_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\end{matrix}.

Proposition
3.6
implies that the
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing induces a Banach space isomorphism
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)\cong\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)^{*}
. The fact that
\scalebox{1.5}{a}:\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)\to\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger})
is an isomorphism follows from Corollary
4.8
.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

If
D
is an elliptic differential operator of order
m=1
with adapted boundary operator
A
, the results of
[
8
]
shows that

\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\chi^{-}(A^{\ast}){\rm H}^{\rm-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E)\oplus\chi^{+}(A^{\ast}){\rm H}^{\rm{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E).

We use the notation
\hat{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)
for the right hand side of this equation. The reader should be aware that in
[
8
]
, this space was denoted by
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(A^{\ast})
.

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
. The boundary pairing

\omega_{D}:\check{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)\to\mathbb{C}.

takes the form

\omega_{D}(\xi,\xi^{\prime})=\langle\xi,\scalebox{1.5}{a}\xi^{\prime}\rangle_{{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})},\quad\xi\in\check{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger}),\;\xi^{\prime}\in\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),

and is a perfect pairing.

Proof.

The formula for the boundary pairing for smooth sections follows from Proposition
2.15
and that it can be written as the stated
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing follows from Lemma
4.10
.
∎

4.3.
Adjoint boundary conditions and a sufficient condition for regularity

In light of the results of last subsection, we can further describe adjoint boundary conditions.
In particular, we can derive a sufficient condition for certain semi-regular boundary conditions to be regular.

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
and
B\subseteq\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
a generalised boundary condition. The adjoint boundary condition
B^{*}\subseteq\check{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger})
takes the form

B^{*}=(\scalebox{1.5}{a}B)^{\perp}=\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}^{-1}B^{\perp},

where
\perp
denotes the induced annihilators with respect to
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing.

Proof.

Using the description in Lemma
4.10
, the space
(\scalebox{1.5}{a}B)^{\perp}\subseteq\check{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger})
is the annihilator of
\scalebox{1.5}{a}B\subseteq\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger})
in the perfect
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger})\times\check{\mathrm{H}}(D^{\dagger})\to\mathbb{C}
. By Theorem
4.12
,
(\scalebox{1.5}{a}B)^{\perp}
is the annihilator of
B
in the boundary pairing
\omega_{D}
, so
B^{*}=(\scalebox{1.5}{a}B)^{\perp}
follows. By a similar argument,
\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}B^{*}\subseteq\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
is the annihilator of
B\subseteq\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
in the
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing, so
\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}B^{*}=B^{\perp}
which implies
B^{*}=\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}^{-1}B^{\perp}
.
∎

The reader should compare Proposition
4.13
to Proposition
2.26
describing the adjoint boundary condition of a pseudo-local boundary condition. We shall now state a theorem providing a rather general sufficient condition for regularity.

Theorem 4.14
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
and
P
a continuous projection on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
that extends by continuity to a projection on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Assume that the operator
A:=P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P)
defines a Fredholm operator on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
that extends by continuity to a Fredholm operator on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Then
P
defines an regular boundary condition

B_{P}:=(1-P){\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),

for
D
. In other words, we have that

\{u\in\mathrm{dom}(D_{\rm max}):P\gamma u=0\}=\{u\in{\rm H}^{\rm m}(M;E):P\gamma u=0\},

and this space defines the domain of an regular realisation of
D
.

Proof.

By using the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
3.30
, we see that if
A:=P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P)
is Fredholm on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
, then

\|(1-P)u\|_{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}\simeq\|(1-P)u\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})},

for
u\in{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. In particular,
B
is closed in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
. This proves that
B
is a semi-regular boundary condition. It remains to prove that
B^{*}
is a semi-regular boundary condition.

We claim that
B^{*}=(1-P_{\dagger}){\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and that this is a semi-regular boundary condition. Here we use the notation
P_{\dagger}:=\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}^{-1}(1-P^{*})\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}
, where
P^{*}:{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
is the continuous projection defined from the
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing
{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\to\mathbb{C}
. By the arguments in the preceding paragraph and Proposition
2.26
, this claim follows if
A_{\dagger}:=P_{\mathcal{C}^{\dagger}}-(1-P_{\dagger})
is Fredholm on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. However, using Theorem
4.6
we have that

A_{\dagger}=\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}^{-1}A^{*}\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger},

where we by an abuse of notation write
A
for its Fredholm extension to
{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
A^{*}
for its
{\rm L}^{2}
-adjoint on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
. The fact that
A_{\dagger}
is Fredholm now follows from that
A
is Fredholm on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
∎

The next result specialises Theorem
4.14
to pseudolocal boundary conditions and characterises those that are regular. We encourage the reader to compare the assumptions on
P
in the next corollary to those in Corollary
3.32
.

Theorem 4.15
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
and
P\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
a projection. Define the generalised boundary condition

B_{P}:=(1-P){\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}).

Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

The operator
P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P)\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
is elliptic.

(2)

B_{P}
is an regular boundary condition for
D
.

(3)

B_{P}
is a Fredholm boundary condition for
D
.

We remark that the space
B_{P}
defined in the theorem above is a priori different from the pseudo-local boundary condition defined from
P
as in Definition
2.24
. A posteriori, they produce the same boundary condition under any of the assumptions i)-iii) in Theorem
4.15
.

Proof.

Item i) implies item ii) by Theorem
4.14
. Item ii) implies item iii) by Proposition
2.6
. Using
[
50
, Theorem XIX.5.1 and XIX.5.2]
, to prove that iii) implies i) it suffices to prove that
P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P)
is Fredholm as soon as
B_{P}
is Fredholm boundary condition. We note that
B_{P}
is Fredholm if and only if
B_{P}^{*}
is Fredholm if and only if
(P_{\mathcal{C}}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),B_{P})
and
((1-P_{\mathcal{C}}^{*})\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B_{P}^{\perp})
are Fredholm pairs. As such,
P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P)
is Fredholm whenever
B_{P}
is a Fredholm boundary condition by combining
[
8
, Lemma A.9]
with Atkinson’s theorem.
∎

To add some more flavor to the regularity condition on a pseudo-local boundary condition as in Theorem
4.15
, let us compare this to the well studied notion of Shapiro-Lopatinskii ellipticity.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
and
B
a pseudo-local boundary condition defined from a pseudo-differential projection
P\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. We say that
P
is Shapiro-Lopatinskii elliptic if for any
(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\in T^{*}\Sigma
, when the differential equation

\begin{cases}\sigma_{\partial}(D)(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})v(t)=0,\quad t>0,\\
\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P)(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\left(D^{j}_{t}v(0)\right)_{j=0}^{m-1}=h,\end{cases}

(12)

has a solution it is uniquely determined and exponentially decaying.

Compare the definition of Shapiro-Lopatinskii elliptic pseudo-differential projectors the the construction of
E_{+}(D)
in Proposition
3.1
. The reader can find more on Shapiro-Lopatinskii ellipticity of pseudo-local boundary conditions in
[
63
]
. Similarly, for more general boundary conditions, if for any
(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\in T^{*}\Sigma
, the differential equation

\begin{cases}\sigma_{\partial}(D)(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})v(t)=0,\quad t>0,\\
\sigma(b)(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\left(D^{j}_{t}v(0)\right)_{j=0}^{m-1}=h,\end{cases}

has a unique exponentially decaying solution (for a suitably defined symbol
\sigma(b)
) for any right hand side
h
, the problem is said to be Shapiro-Lopatinskii elliptic. The reader can find more on Shapiro-Lopatinskii ellipticity of a local boundary condition in
[
3
]
. The constructions in
[
3
]
show how local considerations at the boundary prove to be a highly efficient tool for determining regularity of a local boundary condition. Indeed for the purpose of verifying regularity of local boundary conditions, local semiclassical methods are more direct than the global perspective on a boundary condition as a subspace
B\subseteq\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
that dominates this paper.

Example 4.17
.

We return to the study of Dirichlet and Neumann conditions on a second order elliptic differential operator
D
acting on a vector bundle
E
with scalar positive principal symbol as in Example
3.34
. We have that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P_{D}))(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}1_{E}&|\xi|^{\prime-1}1_{E}\\
|\xi^{\prime}|1_{E}&-1_{E}\end{pmatrix}\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P_{N}))(x^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}-1_{E}&|\xi|^{\prime-1}1_{E}\\
|\xi^{\prime}|1_{E}&1_{E}\end{pmatrix}.

It follows that
P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P_{D})
and
P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P_{N})
are elliptic. Therefore Theorem
4.15
reproves the well known fact that Dirichlet and Neumann conditions are regular. A short computation (or using Proposition
4.18
below) reproves the well known fact that Dirichlet and Neumann conditions are Shapiro-Lopatinskii elliptic. By Example
3.34
, the projectors
P_{D}
and
P_{N}
are not boundary decomposing which shows that regularity of a boundary condition defined from a projection is not equivalent to the boundary decomposing property of a projector.

We now return to the general case of pseudo-local boundary conditions defined from a projection in the Douglis-Nirenberg calculus.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
and
P\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Then
P
is Shapiro-Lopatinskii elliptic if and only if
P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P)\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
is elliptic.

In particular, a Shapiro-Lopatinskii elliptic pseudo-local boundary condition is regular in the sense of Subsection
2.2
.

Proof.

The condition of Shapiro-Lopatinskii ellipticity is equivalent to
\sigma_{0}(P)
defining an isomorphism between
E_{+}(D)
and
\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P)(E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. By
[
8
, Proof of Theorem 2.15]
, this is equivalent to
p_{+}(D)-\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(1-P)
being an isomorphism in all points, or in other words that
P_{\mathcal{C}}-(1-P)
is elliptic.
∎

5.
Characterisations of regularity via graphical decompositions

A graphical characterisation for regular boundary conditions was first given in
[
7
]
, motivated by the desire to capture regular boundary conditions beyond those that are given by pseudodifferential projectors.
The main application in mind was a remarkably conceptual proof of the relative index theorem, originally due to Gromov-Lawson
[
34
]
.
In
[
7
]
, the boundary decomposing projectors were assumed to be self-adjoint spectral projectors, with the assumption that the adapted operator on the boundary was self-adjoint.
A particular luxury in this setting was that the obtained decomposition could be equated with orthogonal complements in
{\rm L}^{2}
.
This was subsequently generalised
[
8
, Theorem 2.9]
for general first order operators, where orthogonal complements were no longer available and the decomposition had to be obtained separately for the boundary condition and its adjoint.
In particular, this meant that there were double the number of spaces in the decomposition and we consider the most significant step towards our presentation here for the general order case.

5.1.
Preliminaries

As above, we assume that
D
is an elliptic differential operator and
\mathcal{P}_{+}
is a boundary decomposing projection for
D
(in the sense of Definition
3.26
on page
3.26
). We use the notation
\mathcal{P}_{-}=1-\mathcal{P}_{+}
and
\mathcal{P}_{+}^{*}
for the appropriate dual operator to
\mathcal{P}_{+}
with respect to the duality induced by
{\rm L}^{2}
inner product.

Lemma 5.1
.

The pairing

\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:{\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{\pm}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\alpha}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n}_{\rm op})\times\mathcal{P}_{\pm}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\alpha}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\to\mathbb{C},

induced from the
{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)
inner product is a perfect pairing for
\alpha\in\{-\frac{1}{2},m-\frac{1}{2}\}
.

Proof.

The
{\rm L}^{2}
-inner product
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
induces a perfect pairing between
{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\alpha}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n}_{\rm op})
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\alpha}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
by Proposition
3.6
.
The desired conclusion follows simply on making the right identifications to the spaces
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
,
X
,
Y
in Lemma
A.1
.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

For any boundary decomposing projection
\mathcal{P}_{+}
for the elliptic differential operator
D
, we have that:

(1)

\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\mathcal{P}_{-}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and

(2)

\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\oplus\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
.

Proof.

Item
1
follows from Proposition
3.27
. The proof of
2
follows from
1
since
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\oplus\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
, Lemma
5.1
, and
[
55
, Theorem 4.8 (4.12) in Chapter 4, Section 4]
.
∎

Definition 5.3

(Graphical decomposition)
.

Let
B
be a boundary condition and
\mathcal{P}_{+}
a boundary decomposing projection for an elliptic differential operator
D
of order
m>0
. Following
[
8
]
, we say that a graphical decomposition of
B
(with respect to
\mathcal{P}_{+}
) is a decomposition of the following form:

(1)

there exist mutually complementary subspaces
W_{\pm}
and
V_{\pm}
of
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
satisfying:

W_{\pm}\oplus V_{\pm}=\mathcal{P}_{\pm}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),

(2)

it holds that
{W_{-}}^{\ast}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
, and
{W_{\pm}}^{\ast}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
and the subspaces
W_{\pm}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
are finite dimensional,

(3)

there exists a continuous map
g:V_{-}\to V_{+}
such that

{g}^{\ast}({V}^{\ast}_{+}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))\subset{V}^{\ast}_{-}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}),

where
{g}^{\ast}
denotes the adjoint in the induced
{\rm L}^{2}
-pairing between
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})

B=\left\{v+gv:v\in V_{-}\right\}\oplus W_{+}.

If a boundary condition admits a graphical decomposition (with respect to
\mathcal{P}_{+}
), we say it is
\mathcal{P}_{+}
-graphically decomposable.

We remark that spaces
V_{\pm}^{*},W_{\pm}^{\ast}
, denotes subspaces of
{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
obtained via the induced pairing from the
{\rm L}^{2}
-inner product, formed with respect to the direct sum decomposition
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=V_{+}\oplus W_{+}\oplus V_{-}\oplus W_{-}
.

Definition 5.4

(
\mathcal{P}_{+}
Fredholm pair decomposed in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}
)
.

Let
B\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Suppose:

(1)

B
is closed subspace of
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

(2)

(\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),B)
and
(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))
are Fredholm pairs in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))
, respectively, and

(3)

\indx(\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E),B)=-\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})).

Then, we say that
B
is
Fredholm-pair decomposed with respect to
\mathcal{P}_{+}
in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}
.

Definition 5.5

(
\mathcal{P}_{+}
Fredholm pair decomposed in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
)
.

Let
B\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Suppose:

(1)

B
is closed subspace of
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
and
B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
is a closed subspace of
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
,

(2)

(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),B)
and
(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))
are Fredholm pairs in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
and
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
respectively,

(3)

\indx(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),B)=-\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})).

Then, we say that
B
is
Fredholm-pair decomposed with respect to
\mathcal{P}_{+}
in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.

Note that the
\perp
appearing in these definitions taken with respect to the induced pairing from
{\rm L}^{2}
between
{\mathbb{H}}^{\frac{1}{2}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

5.2.
Decomposability

The main theorem we prove in this section is the following.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
. For
\mathcal{P}_{+}
being a boundary decomposing projection for
D
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

B\subset\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
is a regular boundary condition,

(2)

B
is
\mathcal{P}_{+}
graphically decomposable,

(3)

B
is
\mathcal{P}_{+}
Fredholm decomposable in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}

(4)

B
is
\mathcal{P}_{+}
Fredholm decomposable in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.

If one of these equivalent conditions are satisfied, then

\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}{B}^{\ast}=B^{\perp}=\left\{u-{g}^{\ast}u:u\in{V}^{\ast}_{+}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\right\}\oplus{W}^{\ast}_{-}.

(13)

and

\displaystyle\indx(\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),B)

\displaystyle=\indx(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),B),

(14)

\displaystyle\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))

\displaystyle=\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})).

In particular, the properties of being graphically decomposable, Fredholm decomposable in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}
and Fredholm decomposable in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
are independent of the choice of boundary decomposing projection.

The proof of this theorem is aided by the structure of
[
8
, Theorem 2.9]
which deals with the first order case.
In a sense, the essential ingredients are contained in that proof, although the subtleties of this decomposition requires significant care.
For that purpose, we give full details of the argument for the general order case. Before entering the proof of Theorem
5.6
, let us give an index theoretical consequence. The following corollary follows directly from Theorem
2.12
and Equation (
14
)

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
and
B\subseteq{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
a regular boundary condition for
D
. Write
\mathcal{C}_{D}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}:=P_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=\mathcal{C}_{D}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
. The index of
D_{B}
is given by

\indx(D_{B})=\indx(B,\mathcal{C}_{D}^{m-\frac{1}{2}})+\dim\ker(D_{\min})-\dim\ker(D_{\min}^{\dagger}).

in terms of an index of the Fredholm pair
(B,\mathcal{C}_{D}^{m-\frac{1}{2}})
in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

Proof that
2
\implies

1
in Theorem
5.6
.

We shall prove that graphical decomposability implies regularity. First, note that a simple calculation yields

\left\{v+gv:v\in V_{-}\right\}^{\perp,\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}=W_{-}^{\ast}\oplus W_{+}^{\ast}\oplus\left\{u-g^{\ast}u:u\in V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\right\}.

(15)

Moreover, we have that
{\mathbb{H}}^{m-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=W_{-}\oplus V_{-}\oplus W_{+}\oplus V_{+}
, and by Lemma
5.1
, and using Lemma
A.1
, we have that

W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}=V_{-}^{\ast}\oplus W_{-}^{\ast}\oplus V_{+}^{\ast}.

Therefore,

W_{+}^{\perp,\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}=W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}\cap\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)=V_{-}^{\ast}\oplus W_{-}^{\ast}\oplus(V_{+}^{\ast}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})).

(16)

By
[
55
, Theorem 4.8 (4.12) in Chapter 4, Section 4]
, we have that

\displaystyle B^{\perp}

\displaystyle=(W_{+}\oplus\left\{v+gv:v\in V_{-}\right\})^{\perp,\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}

\displaystyle=W_{+}^{\perp,\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}\cap\left\{v+gv:v\in V_{-}\right\}^{\perp,\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}

\displaystyle=W_{-}^{\ast}\oplus\left\{u-g^{\ast}u:u\in V_{-}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\right\},

where the ultimate equality follows from combining (
15
) and (
16
).
Note that by Definition
5.3
, we have that
B^{\perp}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
Proposition
4.13
yields that

\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}{B}^{\ast}=B^{\perp}=\left\{u-{g}^{\ast}u:u\in{V}^{\ast}_{+}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\right\}\oplus{W}^{\ast}_{-}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}),

and therefore, we have that
{B}^{\ast}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;F\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
so
B
is regular.
∎

The proof of
1
\implies

2
is considerably more involved. In order to proceed with the proof
1
\implies

2
, the spaces in the Definition
5.3
need to be defined.
Taking inspiration from
[
8
]
, we define the following spaces:

\displaystyle W_{-}^{\ast}:={\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\cap B^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

(17)

\displaystyle W_{+}:=\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap B

\displaystyle V_{-}^{\ast}:={\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}

\displaystyle V_{+}:=\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

Note that by definition
W_{-}^{\ast}\subset{\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
.
Therefore, we are able to consider its annihilator
(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}
in
{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Recalling the definition of the hybrid spaces

{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=\oplus_{j=0}^{m-1}{\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}-j}(\Sigma;E)\supset\oplus_{j=0}^{m-1}{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}-m+j}(\Sigma;E)={\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}).

Therefore,
V_{-}^{\ast}
is well-defined.
Similarly, it is readily verified that

{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}),

and therefore, we have that
V_{+}
is well defined.

This then allows us to define the remaining spaces as follows simply as the ranges of dual projectors or, as given by Lemma
A.1
, annihilators with respect to the corresponding duality.

\displaystyle W_{+}^{\ast}:={\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\cap V_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

(18)

\displaystyle W_{-}:=\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap(V_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}

\displaystyle V_{+}^{\ast}:={\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

\displaystyle V_{-}:=\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}

Lemma 5.8
.

Assume that
B
is regular and that
V_{+}
,
V_{-}
,
W_{+}
and
W_{-}
are defined as in the paragraphs above.
The following hold:

(1)

W_{\pm}\subseteq{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
W_{\pm}^{\ast}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
are finite dimensional.
In particular,
W_{-}^{\ast}={\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\cap B^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
.

(2)

\mathcal{P}_{-}B\subseteq{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}\subseteq{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
are closed subspaces.

(3)

All of the subspaces
V_{+}
,
V_{-}
,
W_{+}
and
W_{-}
as well as
V_{+}^{\ast}
,
V_{-}^{\ast}
,
W_{+}^{\ast}
and
W_{-}^{\ast}
listed in (
17
) and (
18
) are closed in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
respectively.

(4)

\mathcal{P}_{\pm}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=V_{\pm}\oplus W_{\pm}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\pm}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})=V_{\pm}^{\ast}\oplus W_{\pm}^{\ast}
.

Proof.

Consider
\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}}:B^{\perp}\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
By definition, we have that
B^{\perp}
is closed in
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.
Set
X=(B^{\perp},\|\cdot\|_{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)})
,
Y={\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
,
Z={\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
and the map
K=\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}}:X\to Z
.
For for
b^{\perp}\in B^{\perp}

\|b^{\perp}\|_{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}\simeq\|\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}}b^{\perp}\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}+\|\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}}b^{\perp}\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}.

Note that

\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}}:B^{\perp}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{compact}}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})

and so
on invoking
[
8
, Lemma A.1]
with this choice of
X,Y,Z
and
K
yields that
\ker\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}}
is finite dimensional and
P_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}
is closed in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\ker\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}}

\displaystyle=\left\{b^{\perp}\in B^{\perp}:\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}b^{\perp}=0\right\}=\left\{b^{\perp}\in B^{\perp}:b^{\perp}\in\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)=0\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0001pt=B^{\perp}\cap\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=B^{\perp}\cap\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}),

where the penultimate equality follows from Proposition
5.2

1
and the ultimate equality from the fact that
B^{\perp}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.

Similarly, consider the map
\mathcal{P}_{-}{{\lvert}}_{B}:B\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Since
B\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
, we have that whenever
b\in B
,

\|b\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\simeq\|b\|_{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}\simeq\|\mathcal{P}_{-}{{\lvert}}_{B}b\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}+\|\mathcal{P}_{+}{{\lvert}}_{B}b\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

However,

\mathcal{P}_{+}{{\lvert}}_{B}:B\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\xhookrightarrow{\mathrm{compact}}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),

and therefore, on invoking
[
8
, Lemma A.1]
yields that
W_{+}=\ker(\mathcal{P}_{+}{{\lvert}}_{B})
is finite dimensional and
\mathcal{P}_{-}B
is closed in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

Therefore, we have proven the property
2
as well as a part of the property
1
, namely that
W_{-}^{\ast}
and
W_{+}
are finite dimensional and that the asserted equality for
W_{-}^{\ast}
holds.
We prove that
W_{-}
and
W_{-}^{\ast}
are finite dimensional after proving
3
and
4
.

For that, we first prove that
\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=V_{-}^{\ast}\oplus W_{-}^{\ast}
.
From the formula for
W_{-}^{\ast}
that we have already proven, we have that
W_{-}^{\ast}\subset\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
and it is closed since it is finite dimensional.
By construction
V_{-}^{\ast}\subset\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
Therefore, we have that
V_{-}^{\ast}+W_{-}^{\ast}\subset\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.

Next, we show that
V_{-}^{\ast}\cap W_{-}^{\ast}=0
.
Fix
w\in V_{-}^{\ast}\cap W_{-}^{\ast}=\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap W_{-}^{\ast}
.
In particular, we have that
w\in(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap W_{-}^{\ast}
.
Since
w\in W_{-}^{\ast}
we also have that
w\in{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
which is in a perfect pairing with
{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Therefore, we have that
\left\langle w,w\right\rangle=0
.
Moreover, it is readily verified that
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\subset\oplus_{j=0}^{m-1}{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)
and therefore,

\|w\|_{{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)}^{2}=\left\langle w,w\right\rangle_{{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)}=\left\langle w,w\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}=0,

from which we conclude that
w=0
.
This proves that
V_{-}^{\perp}\oplus W_{-}^{\perp}\subset\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.

We now prove the reverse containment.
For that, let
k=\dim W_{-}^{\ast}
and fix a basis
e_{1},\dots,e_{k}
.
By considering each
e_{i}\in\oplus_{j=0}^{m-1}{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)
, we can run the Gram-Schmidt process to obtain an
{\rm L}^{2}
-orthonormal basis
\tilde{e}_{i}
.
Therefore, without the loss of generality, assume that
\left\{e_{i}\right\}
are orthonormal in
{\rm L}^{2}
.
For
u\in{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
, define

Pu=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\langle u,e_{i}\right\rangle e_{i}.

It is readily checked that this is a projector and in fact,
P:{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
with
P{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=W_{-}^{\ast}
and
\ker P=(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}
.
Therefore, restricting to
u\in\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
, we obtain that
u=Pu-(1-P)u
and
Pu\in W_{-}^{\ast}
.
This means that
(1-P)u\in(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
since
W_{-}^{\ast}\subset\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
and therefore,
(1-P)u\in V_{-}^{\ast}
.
Hence, we conclude that
V_{-}^{\perp}\oplus W_{-}^{\perp}=\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
By exploiting the duality yielded in Lemma
5.1
, we conclude that
V_{-},W_{-}
are closed in
\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Via Lemma
A.1
, we obtain
\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=V_{-}\oplus W_{-}
.

A similar argument then holds to show that
\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=V_{+}\oplus W_{+}
.
Showing
V_{+}+W_{+}\subset\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and that
V_{+}\cap W_{+}=0
is an easy calculation.
The most important point to show the reverse containment is note that
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
This allows for the construction of a projector
Q:{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
with
Q{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=W_{+}
and
\ker Q=W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}
and consequently, write
\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\subset V_{+}\oplus W_{+}
.
As before, this yields that
V_{+}^{\ast}
and
W_{+}^{\ast}
are closed subspaces of
\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
and Lemma
A.1
then yields
\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=V_{+}^{\ast}\oplus W_{+}^{\ast}
.

This proves
3
and
4
.
It remains to show that
W_{+}^{\ast}
and
W_{-}
are finite dimensional.
For this, note that by using Lemma
A.1
, we have induced perfect pairings
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:W_{+}\times W_{+}^{\ast}\to\mathbb{C}
and
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:W_{-}\times W_{-}^{\ast}\to\mathbb{C}
.
But that means that
W_{+}^{\prime}\cong W_{+}^{\ast}
and
W_{-}^{\prime}\cong W_{-}^{\ast}
, where
W_{+}^{\prime}
and
W_{-}^{\prime}
are dual spaces of
W_{+}
and
W_{-}
respectively.
By finite dimensionality we have that
W_{+}^{\prime}\cong W_{+}
and
W_{-}^{\prime}\cong W_{-}
and this finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
5.8
, the following hold:

(1)

V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}
, and

(2)

V_{-}=\mathcal{P}_{-}B
.

Proof.

First, we prove that

W_{+}=(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}).

(19)

Then,

\displaystyle x\in(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}

\displaystyle\iff 0=\left\langle x,\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}b^{\perp}\right\rangle\ \forall b^{\perp}\in B^{\perp}

\displaystyle\iff 0=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{+}x,b^{\perp}\right\rangle\ \forall b^{\perp}\in B^{\perp}

\displaystyle\implies\mathcal{P}_{+}x\in(B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

Therefore,
x\in(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
implies that
x=\mathcal{P}_{+}x
.
Moreover, since by Proposition
5.2

\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
B^{\perp}=B^{\perp,\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
, we obtain that

B=(B^{\perp,\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)})^{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}=(B^{\perp})^{{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.
Therefore,
x\in(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
then yields that
x\in\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
and hence,
x\in B
.
This shows ,

(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\subset B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=W_{+}.

For the reverse containment, fix
w\in W_{+}=B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Therefore, for all
b^{\perp}\in B^{\perp}
, we have that

\displaystyle 0=\left\langle w,b^{\perp}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{+}w,b^{\perp}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle w,\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}b^{\perp}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}.

Therefore, we have
w\in(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}
and by assumption
w\in\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
That is precisely that
w\in(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

Now, by the definition of
V_{+}^{\ast}
in (
18
), we have that

\displaystyle V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

By the description of
W_{+}
in (
19
) and on using
[
55
, Theorem 4.8 (4.12) in Chapter 4, Section 4]
, we obtain

W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}+(\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}))^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\cap(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}.

This proves
1
.

The proof of
2
is similar.
First, we prove that

W_{-}^{\ast}=(\mathcal{P}_{-}B)^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}).

(20)

Recall that by Lemma
5.8

1
,
W_{-}^{\ast}={\mathcal{P}}^{\ast}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\cap B^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}-m}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
.
and so for
w\in W_{-}^{\ast}
, we have that for all
b\in B
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\left\langle w,b\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}w,b\right\rangle=\left\langle w,\mathcal{P}_{-}b\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\times{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}

This shows the containment “
\subset
”.
For the reverse containment, let
x\in(\mathcal{P}_{-}B)^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}\cap\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
Arguing similarly, for all
b\in B
,

0=\left\langle x,\mathcal{P}_{-}b\right\rangle=\left\langle\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}x,b\right\rangle

which yields that
x=\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}x\in B^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
.

With this, we now note that

\displaystyle V_{-}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap(\mathcal{P}_{-}B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap\mathcal{P}_{-}B

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{-}B.\qed

Proof that
1
\implies

2
in Theorem
5.6
.

We shall prove that regularity implies graphical decomposability. We use the spaces
V_{+}
,
V_{-}
,
W_{+}
and
W_{-}
constructed on the preceding pages.
Since
B\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
, we have that
W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}\cap B\neq 0
.
Define

X_{-}:=\mathcal{P}_{-}{{\lvert}}_{B\cap W_{+}^{\perp}}:B\cap W_{+}^{\perp}\to\mathcal{P}_{-}B.

We prove that this map is a bijection and hence, by the open mapping theorem, it is a Banach space isomorphism between
(B,\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})})
and
(\mathcal{P}_{-}B,\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})})
.
First, we establish surjectivity of
X_{-}
.
For that, note that

{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=W_{+}\oplus W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})},

(21)

which follows from a construction of a projector as in the proof of
3
in Lemma
5.8
.
Let
P_{W_{+},W_{+}^{\perp}}
denote the projection to
W_{+}
along
W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
and the complementary projector
P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}
.
Fix
x=P_{-}b\in\mathcal{P}_{-}B
and note

x=P_{-}(P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}b+P_{W_{+},W_{+}^{\perp}}b)=P_{-}P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}b.

Since
W_{+}\subset B
,
P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}b=b-P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}b\in B
and hence
P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}b\in B\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
.
This shows surjectivity.
Injectivity follows easily by taking
0=X_{-}b=P_{-}b
which allows us to conclude

b\in\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap B\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}=W_{+}\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}=0.

Define projectors
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
as:

P_{+}:=P_{V_{+},W_{+}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\quad\text{and}\quad Q_{+}:=P_{W_{+},V_{+}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

Furthermore, note that since
V_{-}
has an induced Hilbert structure and
\mathcal{P}_{-}B\subset V_{-}
is closed, we obtain a closed complementary subspace
V_{-}^{\prime}
such that
V_{-}=\mathcal{P}_{-}B\oplus V_{-}^{\prime}
.
Using the fact that
X_{-}:(B,\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})})\to(\mathcal{P}_{-}B,\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})})
is an isomorphism, and the fact that
V_{-}=\mathcal{P}_{-}B
from Lemma
5.9

2
, define the
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
-bounded map
g_{0}:V_{-}\to V_{+}
by setting

g_{0}(x)=P_{+}\,\circ\,(X_{-})^{-1}.

First, we prove that
W_{+}\oplus\left\{u+g_{0}u:u\in\mathcal{P}_{-}B\right\}\subset B
.
By construction, we have that
W_{+}\subset B
, so it remains to show
\left\{u+g_{0}u:u\in V_{-}\right\}\subset B
.
For that, we compute

\displaystyle u+g_{0}u

\displaystyle=u+P_{+}\,\circ\,(X_{-})^{-1}u

\displaystyle=X_{-}v+P_{+}v

\displaystyle=X_{-}v+P_{+}v+Q_{+}v-Q_{+}v

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{-}v+\mathcal{P}_{+}v-Q_{+}v

\displaystyle=v-Q_{+}v\in B\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}+W_{+}\subset B,

where we have set
v:=(X_{-})^{-1}u
.

We now prove the reverse containment that
B\subset W_{+}\oplus\left\{u+g_{0}u:u\in V_{-}\right\}
.
Via the decomposition (
21
), we have a induced decomposition

{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=W_{+}\oplus(W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})).

This decomposition holds because
W_{+}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
and for the same reason, the projectors for this decomposition are the projectors
P_{W_{+},W_{+}^{\perp}}
and
P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}
restricted to
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
For
b\in B\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
, let
b^{\perp}=P_{W_{+}^{\perp},W_{+}}b
and
b_{+}=P_{W_{+},W_{+}^{\perp}}b
, so that
b=b^{\perp}+b_{+}
.
It is easy to see that, since
W_{+}\subset B
,
b^{\perp}\in B\cap W_{+}^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
.
Let
v:=X_{-}b^{\perp}
.
Then,

\displaystyle b^{\perp}=(X_{-})^{-1}v

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{-}(X_{-})^{-1}v+\mathcal{P}_{+}(X_{-})^{-1}v=v+P_{+}(X_{-})^{-1}v+Q_{+}(X_{-})^{-1}v

\displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt=v+g_{0}v+Q_{+}(X_{-})^{-1}v\in\left\{u+g_{0}u:u\in\mathcal{P}_{-}B\right\}\oplus W_{+}.

Let

X_{+}^{\ast}:=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{B^{\perp}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}}:B^{\perp}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\to\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}.

Note that, by using a similar projector construction as in the proof of Lemma
5.8

2
, we obtain

{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=W_{-}^{\ast}\oplus(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

(22)

As before,
w\in W_{-}^{\ast}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}
yields
w=0
since, in particular,
w\in\oplus_{j=0}^{m-1}{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)
and therefore,

\left\langle w,w\right\rangle=\|w\|^{2}_{{\rm L}^{2}}
.
For
x=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}b^{\perp}\in\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}
, we obtain
x=\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}P_{W_{-}^{\ast},(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp}}b^{\perp}
.
As before, since
W_{-}^{\ast}\subset B^{\perp}
, we have that
P_{W_{-}^{\ast},(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp}}b^{\perp}\in B^{\perp}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}
which establishes the surjectivity of
X_{+}^{\ast}
.
Injectivity follows simply on noting that
X_{+}^{\ast}x=0
implies

x\in\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap B^{\perp}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}=W_{-}^{\ast}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}=0.

This shows that
X_{+}^{\ast}
is a surjection and by the open mapping theorem, it is an isomorphism between
(B^{\perp}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})},\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})})
and
(\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp},\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})})
, where the former space was established as a Banach space in Lemma
5.8

2
.

Next, define the following projectors on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
:

P_{-}^{\ast}:=P_{V_{-}^{\ast},W_{-}^{\ast}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}\quad\text{and}\quad Q_{-}^{\ast}:=P_{W_{-}^{\ast},V_{-}^{\ast}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}.

We have that
P_{-}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
is a bounded map from
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
to
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
since

P_{-}^{\ast}u=Q_{-}^{\ast}u-\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}u

and
\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})}
is a bounded map on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
and
Q_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}-m}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})=W_{-}^{\ast}\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
Therefore, using Lemma
5.9

1
to equate
\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}=V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
,

h_{0}:=P_{-}^{\ast}\,\circ\,(X_{+}^{\ast})^{-1}:V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})\to V_{-}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}).

Since
P_{-}^{\ast}
is
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
bounded, the map
X_{+}^{\ast}
was established to be an isomorphism of the respective Banach spaces it maps between, we have that
h_{0}
is bounded in the
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
-norm.

To show
W_{-}^{\ast}\oplus\left\{u+h_{0}u:u\in\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}\right\}\subset B^{\perp}
, we note that
W_{-}^{\ast}\subset B^{\perp}
by construction and that, setting
w=(X_{+}^{\ast})^{-1}u
for
u\in P_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}
,

u+h_{0}u=w-Q_{-}\ast w\in B^{\perp}+W_{-}^{\ast}\subset B^{\perp}.

For the reverse containment, using (
22
), we write
x=x^{\perp}+x_{-}\in B^{\perp}\cap(W_{-}^{\ast})^{\perp,{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}\oplus W_{-}^{\ast}
and letting

w=(X_{+}^{\ast})x^{\perp}
, we deduce that

x^{\perp}=w+h_{0}w+Q_{-}^{\ast}(X_{+}^{\ast})^{-1}w\in\left\{u+h_{0}u:v\in P_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}\right\}\oplus W_{-}^{\ast}.

Lastly, we show that
h_{0}=-g_{0}^{\ast}{{\lvert}}_{V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}
.
For that, we note that
\left\langle B,B^{\perp}\right\rangle=0
in the pairing between
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
and
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
and so therefore,

0=\left\langle u+g_{0}u,v+h_{0}v\right\rangle=\left\langle u,v\right\rangle+\left\langle u,h_{0}v\right\rangle+\left\langle g_{0}u,v\right\rangle+\left\langle g_{0}u,h_{0}v\right\rangle.

However
u\in V_{-}
and
v\in V_{+}^{\ast}
and therefore,

0=\left\langle u,v\right\rangle=\left\langle g_{0}u,h_{0}v\right\rangle.

From this, we obtain that

\left\langle u,h_{0}v\right\rangle=\left\langle u,-g_{0}^{\ast}v\right\rangle

for all
u\in V_{-}
and
v\in V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
.
This shows us that
h_{0}v=-g_{0}^{\ast}v
for all
v\in V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
and moreover, since
h_{0}v\in V_{-}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}})
, by construction of
h_{0}
, we obtain that

g_{0}(V_{+}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}))\subset V_{-}^{\ast}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\mathrm{op}}).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Next, we move onto proving the assertions regarding Fredholm pairs characterisations of regular boundary conditions.

Proof that
2
\implies

3
in Theorem
5.6
.

We shall prove that graphical decomposability implies Fredholm decomposability in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}
.
The argument is exactly as in the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 2.9]
.
The idea is to use
2
to write
B=\left\{v+gv:v\in V_{-}\right\}\oplus W_{+}
and

\scalebox{1.5}{a}_{\dagger}{B}^{\ast}=B^{\perp}=\left\{u-{g}^{\ast}u:u\in{V}^{\ast}_{+}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})\right\}\oplus{W}^{\ast}_{-}.

This, along with Proposition
5.2
yields

\displaystyle B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=W_{+},\quad(B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))\cap\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)=W_{-}^{\ast},

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}{\raisebox{3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}{\raisebox{2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}}\cong W_{-},\text{ and}

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.61111pt}{$\scriptstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.61111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.51909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}}}\cong W_{+}^{\ast}.

Since
W_{\pm}\cong W_{\pm}^{\ast}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

Now, we prove the rest of the theorem in two parts.

Proof that
3
\implies

4
in Theorem
5.6
.

We shall prove that Fredholm decomposability in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}
implies Fredholm decomposability in
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.
The argument is divided into three steps.

(1)

B
is closed in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.

Set
W_{+}:=B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
, since
B\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Therefore, using Proposition
5.2
,

B=W_{+}\oplus(B\cap W_{+}^{\perp})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=W_{+}\oplus(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)\cap W_{+}^{\perp}).

Also,
B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap W_{+}^{\perp}=W_{+}\cap W_{+}^{\perp}=0
, and therefore,

B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=B\oplus(B\cap W_{+}^{\perp}).

Next, we claim that this space is closed.
For that, we use
[
8
, Lemma A.4]
to see that
B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=\mathcal{P}_{-}B\oplus\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
and note that
\mathcal{P}_{-}B\subset\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})=\mathcal{P}_{-}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
is a closed subspace in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.

Now, using that
(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))
is a Fredholm pair in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))
, we obtain that
(\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})),(B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))^{\perp})
is a Fredholm pair in
{\mathbb{H}}^{{-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
with opposite index.
Using Proposition
5.2
and
[
8
, Lemma A.5]
, we obtain that
(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),\check{B})
is a Fredholm pair in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
, where
\check{B}=(B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))^{\perp}
.
This yields
B\oplus(\check{B}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)\cap W_{+}^{\perp})
is closed in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.

To prove the desired claim, we show that
\check{B}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)\cap W_{+}^{\perp}=0
.
For that, it suffices to show that
\check{B}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.
Since
B\subset\check{B}
, and the dimension of
B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
is finite, it suffices to show that
\dim(\check{B}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D))\leq\dim(B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D))
.
For that,

\displaystyle\dim(\check{B}\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D))

\displaystyle=\dim\left({\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.61111pt}{$\scriptstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.61111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.61111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B^{\perp}+\mathcal{P}_{-}^{\ast}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dim\left({\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}{\raisebox{3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}{\raisebox{2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.51909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\dim\left({\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}{\raisebox{3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}{\raisebox{2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.51909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{+}^{\ast}B^{\perp}}$}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\dim(B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}))=\dim(B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)).

(2)

(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),B)
is a Fredholm pair in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
, with

\indx(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),B)=\indx(\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),B).

Contained in the proof of
1
was that
B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
is a closed subspace of
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.
It is clear that
B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
is a closed subspace of
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
.

Using
[
8
, Lemma A.4]
, we obtain that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}{\raisebox{3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}{\raisebox{2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}}

as well as

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B+\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}{\raisebox{3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}{\raisebox{2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{P}_{-}B}$}}}.

This along with the fact that
B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)=B\cap\mathcal{P}_{+}{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
then yields the conclusion.

(3)

B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
is closed in
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
and
(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))
is a Fredholm pair with

\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))=\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})).

That
B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
is closed in
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
is obtained via a repetition of the argument in
1
with the appropriate changes.
Similarly, the remaining conclusion is obtained on mirroring the argument
2
with the necessary changes.
∎

The last remaining part to establish Theorem
5.6
is given below.

Proof that
4
\implies

1
in Theorem
5.6
.

We shall prove that Fredholm decomposability in
\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D)
implies regularity. .
Since
B\subset{\mathbb{H}}^{{m-\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
and closed in
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)
, we only need to show that
B^{\perp}\subseteq{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
.

By hypothesis, we have that
\indx(\mathcal{P}_{+}\check{\mathrm{H}}(D),B)=-\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))
, and therefore
\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op}))=\indx(\mathcal{P}_{-}^{*}\hat{\mathrm{H}}(D),B^{\perp})
.
By
[
8
, Lemma A.7]
, we obtain that
B^{\perp}=B^{\perp}\cap{\mathbb{H}}^{{\frac{1}{2}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\rm op})
.
∎

5.3.
The first order case

For general boundary decomposing projectors
\mathcal{P}_{\pm}
in the first order case, a decomposition more in the spirit of
[
8
]
can be obtained. We remark that if
\mathcal{P}_{+}
is boundary decomposing for a first order elliptic operator, then
\mathcal{P}_{+}
extends by interpolation to
{\rm H}^{\rm s}(\Sigma;E)
for any
s\in[-{\frac{1}{2}},{\frac{1}{2}}]
.

Definition 5.10

(Graphical
{\rm L}^{2}
-decomposition)
.

Let
B
be a boundary condition and
\mathcal{P}_{+}
a boundary decomposing projection for an elliptic differential operator
D
of order
1
. We say that a
graphical
{\rm L}^{2}
-decomposition
(with respect to
\mathcal{P}_{+}
) of
B
is the following decomposition:

(1)

there exist mutually complementary subspaces
W_{\pm}
and
V_{\pm}
of
{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)
satisfying:

W_{\pm}\oplus V_{\pm}=\mathcal{P}_{\pm}{\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E),

(2)

it holds that
W_{\pm}
are finite dimensional with
W_{\pm},{W_{\pm}}^{\ast}\subset{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
, and

(3)

there exists a bounded linear map
g:V_{-}\to V_{+}
such that

{g}^{\ast}({V}^{\ast}_{+}\cap{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E))\subset{V}^{\ast}_{-}\cap{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)\quad\mbox{and}\quad g(V_{-}\cap{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E))\subset V_{+}\cap{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E),

where
{g}^{\ast}
denotes the
{\rm L}^{2}
-adjoint and

B=\left\{v+gv:v\in V_{-}\cap{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)\right\}\oplus W_{+}.

A boundary condition admitting a graphical
{\rm L}^{2}
-decomposition is said to be graphically
{\rm L}^{2}
-decomposable.

As before, the spaces
W_{\pm}^{\ast}
that appear in the definition, as before, are simply the ranges of the dual projectors.
However, our computations are always in
{\rm L}^{2}
, and therefore, this is simply the ranges of the adjoint projectors in
{\rm L}^{2}
with respect to the
{\rm L}^{2}
inner product. The special case
\mathcal{P}_{+}=\chi^{+}(A)
, for an adapted boundary operator
A
, of Definition (
5.10
) was stated in
[
8
, Definition 2.6]
.

We emphasise that in Definition
5.10
, unlike for the graphical decomposition in Definition
5.3
, the decomposition obtained is in
{\rm L}^{2}
.
Indeed, the following theorem asserts that in the first order case, these decompositions are equivalent. The precise equivalence is set up as follows: a graphical
{\rm L}^{2}
-decomposition produces a graphical decomposition as in Definition
5.3
by intersecting with
{\rm H}^{\rm{\frac{1}{2}}}
and the converse is constructed from
{\rm L}^{2}
-closures.
We present this first order definition separately as it can be useful to compute in
{\rm L}^{2}
in the first order case instead of the more complicated spaces that appear in the general order decomposition.

Theorem 5.11
.

For a first order elliptic differential operator
D
, a boundary condition
B
is regular if and only if it is graphically
{\rm L}^{2}
-decomposable.
In this case, we have that

B^{\perp}=\left\{u-{g}^{\ast}u:u\in{V}^{\ast}_{+}\cap{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)\right\}\oplus{W}^{\ast}_{-}.

The proof of this theorem runs as in the proof of
[
8
, Theorem 2.9]
, with the projectors
\chi^{\pm}(A_{r})
replaced by
\mathcal{P}_{\pm}
.

6.
Higher order boundary regularity

We will now prove results on higher order boundary regularity. These results rely on the fact that Theorem
3.18
due to Seeley was, in fact, stated in
[
64
]
also for higher order Sobolev spaces.

6.1.
Preliminaries

For an elliptic differential operator
D
, we let
D_{\rm min,s}
and
D_{\rm max,s}
denote the minimal and maximal, respectively, closures of
D
acting densely defined on
{\rm H}^{\rm s}(M;E)\to{\rm H}^{\rm s}(M;F)
. We let
\gamma_{s}:\mathrm{dom}(D_{\rm max,s})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{s-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
denote the trace mapping.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
D
be an elliptic differential operator of order
m>0
.
The Calderón projection
P_{\mathcal{C}}\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
from Theorem
3.18
satisfies the following.

(1)

The operator
\mathcal{K}
from Theorem
3.18
defines a continuous Poisson operator

\mathcal{K}_{s}:{\mathbb{H}}^{{s-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\to\mathrm{dom}(D_{\rm max,s}),

such that
P_{\mathcal{C}}=\gamma_{s}\circ\mathcal{K}_{s}
on
{\mathbb{H}}^{{s-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

(2)

The image of

\gamma_{s}:\mathrm{dom}(D_{\rm max,s})\to{\mathbb{H}}^{{s-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}),

is precisely the subspace

P_{\mathcal{C}}{\mathbb{H}}^{{s-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m})\oplus(1-P_{\mathcal{C}}){\mathbb{H}}^{{s+m-{\frac{1}{2}}}}(\Sigma;E\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}).

In particular, the trace map
\gamma_{s}
induces an isomorphism of Banach spaces
