may fail to be infinite dimensional in general, we have instead the following.

Corollary 9.13
.

We have that for any spectral cut
r\in\mathbb{R}
(i.e.,
r\in\mathbb{R}
such that
A_{r}:=A-r
is invertible bisectorial) and any pseudo-differential Calderón projection
\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}
,

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{\chi^{+}(A){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{\chi^{+}(A){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}}

are finite dimensional.

Proof.

Using Corollary
3.28
, we have that
\mathcal{C}_{D}^{c}=\ker\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}
defines a regular boundary condition.
Therefore, from Theorem
5.6
with the choice of
\mathcal{P}_{\pm}=\chi^{\pm}(A_{r})
we have that
(\mathcal{C}_{D}^{c},\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E))
is a Fredholm pair in
\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)
.
Therefore,

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}_{D}^{c}+\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{D}^{c}+\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}_{D}^{c}+\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{C}_{D}^{c}+\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}{\raisebox{1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}}}

via Lemma A4 in
[
8
]
.

For the remaining quotient, we invoke Theorem
5.6
with a choice of
\mathcal{P}_{+}=\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}
, and since the generalised APS condition
\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
is a regular boundary condition, we have that
(\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E),{\mathcal{C}_{D}})
is a Fredholm pair in
\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)
.
Therefore, using
[
8
, Lemma A.4]
, we obtain that

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)+{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)+{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.12001pt}{$\scriptstyle{\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)+{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.33333pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)+{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.23112pt}{$\displaystyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.98112pt}{$\textstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.17558pt}{$\scriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.3889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\chi^{+}(A_{r}){\mathcal{C}_{D}}}$}}}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

If a boundary condition
B
defines a realisation with infinite dimensional kernel, then this is equivalent to saying that
B\cap{\mathcal{C}_{D}}
is infinite dimensional.
Although Corollary
9.13
“measures” the amount of
\chi^{+}(A){\rm H}^{\rm-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
in
{\mathcal{C}_{D}}
(as seen through the projection
\mathrm{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{D}}}
), it does not give us information about such a
B
.
The following gives a more precise description in the way of a necessary condition.

Proposition 9.14
.

Let
B
be a boundary condition.
If
\dim\ker(D_{\rm B})=\infty
, then
B\not\subset{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
and
\dim\chi^{+}(A_{r})B=\infty
.

Proof.

If
B\subset{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}
, then by Proposition
2.6
, we have that
\ker(D_{\rm B})
is finite.
Therefore, the contrapositive yields that for
\ker(D_{\rm B})
infinite, we have that
B\not\subset{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
.

Next, assume that
\dim\chi^{+}(A_{r})B<\infty
.
For
u\in B
, we have that
u=\chi^{-}(A_{r})u+\chi^{+}(A_{r})u
, and we always have that
\chi^{-}(A_{r})u\in{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
.
Setting

B^{\prime}:=\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)\oplus\chi^{+}(A_{r})B,

we therefore have an inclusion
B\subset B^{\prime}
. The space
B^{\prime}
is a boundary condition since
\chi^{+}(A_{r})B
was assumed to be finite dimensional.
Now, letting
B_{0}=\chi^{-}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
, the map

\ker(D_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})/\ker(D_{{\rm B}_{0}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{dom}(D_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})/\mathrm{dom}(D_{{\rm B}_{0}})\cong\chi^{+}(A_{r})B,

is an injection given that
\mathrm{dom}(D_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})=\mathrm{dom}(D_{{\rm B}_{0}})+\mathrm{dom}(D_{\chi^{+}(A_{r})B})

This allows us to conclude that

\dim\ker(D_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})/\ker(D_{{\rm B}_{0}})\leq\dim\chi^{+}(A_{r})B,

and since

\ker(D_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})\cong\ker(D_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})/\ker(D_{{\rm B}_{0}})\oplus\ker(D_{{\rm B}_{0}}),

we have that

\dim\ker(D_{\rm B})\leq\dim\ker(D_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})\leq\dim\chi^{+}(A_{r})B+\dim\ker(D_{{\rm B}_{0}})<\infty.

Taking the contrapositive and combining with our earlier deduction that
B\not\subset{\rm H}^{\rm\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma;E)
concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix A
Lemmas on dimensions and subspaces

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
be Banach spaces and suppose that
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle:\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}\to\mathbb{C}
is a perfect pairing.
Suppose that
\mathcal{B}=X\oplus Y
and let
P_{X,Y}
denote the bounded projection
\mathcal{B}\mapsto X
and similarly for
P_{Y,X}=1-P_{X,Y}
.
Let
X^{\ast}=P_{X,Y}^{\ast}\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
, the dual map to
P_{X,Y}
and similarly
Y^{\ast}=P_{Y,X}^{\ast}\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
.
Then, the following hold:

(1)

P_{X^{\ast},Y^{\ast}}=P_{X,Y}^{\ast}
and
P_{Y^{\ast},X^{\ast}}=P_{Y,X}^{\ast}
. In particular
\mathcal{B}^{\ast}=X^{\ast}\oplus Y^{\ast}
.

(2)

The pairing
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
restricts to perfect pairings
X\times X^{\ast}\to\mathbb{C}
, as well as
Y\times Y^{\ast}\to\mathbb{C}
.

(3)

The annihilators

Y^{\perp}:=\left\{b^{\ast}\in\mathcal{B}^{\ast}:\left\langle b,y\right\rangle=0\ \forall y\in Y\right\}=X^{\ast},\ \text{and}\ \quad X^{\perp}=Y^{\ast}.

Proof.

The item
1
is immediate from the definition of the dual projectors.

We prove
2
.
Let
x\in X
and
x^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}
.
Then, there exists
C<\infty
such that

|\left\langle x,x^{\ast}\right\rangle|\leq C\|x\|_{\mathcal{B}}\|x^{\ast}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}=\|P_{X,Y}x\|_{\mathcal{B}}\|P_{X^{\ast},Y^{\ast}}x^{\ast}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}=\|x\|_{X}\|x^{\ast}\|_{X^{\ast}}.

Again, from the fact that
\left\langle\cdot,\cdot\right\rangle
is a perfect paring between
\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B}^{\ast}
, we have a
C_{1}<\infty
such that

\|x\|\leq C_{1}\sup\left\{\frac{|\left\langle x,b^{\ast}\right\rangle|}{\|b^{\ast}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}}:0\neq b^{\ast}\in\mathcal{B}^{\ast}\right\}.

(30)

Now, note that

\left\langle x,b^{\ast}\right\rangle=\left\langle P_{X,Y}x,b^{\ast}\right\rangle=\left\langle x,P_{X^{\ast},Y^{\ast}}b^{\ast}\right\rangle.

(31)

Moreover, there exists
C_{2}
such that

\|P_{X^{\ast},Y^{\ast}}b^{\ast}\|+\|P_{Y^{\ast},X^{\ast}}b^{\ast}\|\leq C_{2}\|b^{\ast}\|,

and therefore,

\frac{1}{\|b^{\ast}\|}\leq C_{2}\frac{1}{\|P_{X^{\ast},Y^{\ast}}b^{\ast}\|}.

(32)

Combining (
31
) and (
32
) and using (
30
), we obtain

|\left\langle x,x^{\ast}\right\rangle|\leq C_{1}C_{2}\sup\left\{\frac{|\left\langle x,b^{\ast}\right\rangle|}{\|P_{X^{\ast},Y^{\ast}}b^{\ast}\|_{\mathcal{B}^{\ast}}}:0\neq P_{X^{\ast},Y^{\ast}}b^{\ast}\in\mathcal{B}^{\ast}\right\}=C_{1}C_{2}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}\\
1=\|x^{\ast}\|_{X^{\ast}}\end{subarray}}|\left\langle x,x^{\ast}\right\rangle|.

A symmetric argument yields that

\|x^{\ast}\|\lesssim\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in X\\
1=\|x\|_{X}\end{subarray}}|\left\langle x,x^{\ast}\right\rangle|.

Lastly, we prove
3
.
First, suppose that that
b^{\ast}\in Y^{\perp}
.
That is,
0=\left\langle b^{\ast},y\right\rangle
for all
y\in Y
.
Since every
y=P_{Y,X}b
for some
b\in\mathcal{B}
,

0=\left\langle P_{Y^{\ast},X^{\ast}}b^{\ast},b\right\rangle
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
.
This implies that
b^{\ast}\in\ker P_{Y^{\ast},X^{\ast}}=X^{\ast}
and therefore,
Y^{\perp}\subset X^{\ast}
.

Conversely, let
x^{\ast}\in X^{\ast}
.
Then, for
y\in Y
, we have that

\left\langle x^{\ast},y\right\rangle=\left\langle P^{\ast}_{X,Y}x^{\ast},y\right\rangle=\left\langle x^{\ast},P_{X,Y}y\right\rangle=0.

That is,
X^{\ast}\subset Y^{\perp}
.
The statement that
X^{\perp}=Y^{\ast}
follows similarly.
∎

Next we prove two results concerning the dimension of certain subspaces.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
P\in\Psi^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{\rm cl}(M;\boldsymbol{E})
be a classical zeroth order pseudo-differential projection in the Douglis-Nirenberg calculus acting on sections of an
\mathbb{R}
-graded Hermitian vector bundle
\boldsymbol{E}
on a closed manifold
\Sigma
with
\dim(\Sigma)>0
. If
\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P)\neq 0,1
, then for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the space
P{\mathbb{H}}^{s}(\Sigma;\boldsymbol{E})
has infinite dimension and infinite codimension.

In particular, in the setting of Lemma
2.28
with
\dim(M)>1
, then for any admissible cut
r\in\mathbb{R}
, the spaces
\chi^{\pm}(A_{r}){\rm L}^{2}(\Sigma;E)
are infinite dimensional.
Moreover, the space
\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)\neq\left\{0\right\}
.

Proof.

We have that
P
acts compactly on
{\mathbb{H}}^{s}(\Sigma;\boldsymbol{E})
if and only if
\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P)\neq 0
. We conclude that
P{\mathbb{H}}^{s}(\Sigma;\boldsymbol{E})
has infinite dimension if and only if
\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P)\neq 0
. The infinite codimensionality of
P{\mathbb{H}}^{s}(\Sigma;\boldsymbol{E})
is by a similar argument equivalent to
\sigma_{\boldsymbol{0}}(P)\neq 1
.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Let
A
be any elliptic first order differential operator acting on sections of a vector bundle
E
on a closed manifold
\Sigma
such that its resolvent set is non-empty and in some point the principal symbol of
A
has an eigenvalue with positive real part. Then there exists two sequences of eigenvalues
(\lambda_{j}^{+})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(\lambda_{j}^{-})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
of
A
such that

\mathrm{Re}(\lambda^{\pm}_{j})\to\pm\infty,\quad\mbox{as $j\to\infty$}.

In particular, in the setting of Lemma
2.28
with
\dim(M)>1
, then for any admissible cut
r\in\mathbb{R}
and an
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the spaces
\chi^{\pm}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm s}(\Sigma;E)
are infinite dimensional.
Moreover, the space
\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)\neq\left\{0\right\}
.

Proof.

Define the zeroth order classical pseudodifferential operator
F:=A(1+A^{*}A)^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}
.
The spectrum of
F
is contained in the unit disc.
From the fact that
the symbol mapping is a
*
-homomorphism, it follows that the spectrum
of
F
contains the spectrum of its symbol, which we will denote
by
\upsigma_{F}
. In fact, the spectrum of
\upsigma_{F}
coincides with the essential
spectrum of
F
.

From the fact that
A
is first order, it follows that

\lambda\in\mathrm{spec}(\upsigma_{A}(x,\xi))
if and only if

-\lambda\in\mathrm{spec}(\upsigma_{A}(x,-\xi))
. This implies the spectrum of

\upsigma_{F}
is symmetric about the imaginary axis. Since the principal symbol of
A
has an eigenvalue with positive real part in some point, it
follows that
F
has essential spectrum on both sides of the
imaginary axis.

Now,
A
is assumed to have non-empty resolvent set, hence its must be discrete by
[
67
]
.
The essential spectrum of
F
consists of
all limits points of the image of the spectrum of
A
under the
homeomorphism
z\rightarrow z(1+|z|^{2})^{-{\frac{1}{2}}}
of the complex
plane onto the open unit disc. Since
F
has essential spectrum on both sides of
the imaginary axis, the spectrum of
A
has limit points on both
sides of the imaginary axis. It follows that the spectrum of
A
must go
to infinity on both sides of the imaginary axis.
By
[
67
]
, each element of the spectrum is an eigenvalue and the statement follows.

Now, we have that each eigenspace
\mathrm{Eig}(\lambda_{i})\subset{\rm C}^{\infty}(\Sigma;E)
and therefore,
\chi^{\pm}(A_{r}){\rm H}^{\rm s}(\Sigma;E)
are infinite-dimensional. Therefore
\check{\mathrm{H}}_{A}(D)\neq{0}
.
∎

Appendix B
Bär-Ballmann’s approach from an abstract perspective

Although the main results of this paper concern elliptic differential operators on manifolds with boundary, a large proportion of the main tools can be formulated in a general abstract formalism. We believe several of these results to be known to specialists, but nevertheless have decided to formulate the Bär-Ballmann approach in complete abstraction. Similar ideas to those appearing in this appendix can be found in
[
13
]
. In particular, several of our main results have abstract counterparts that hold in potentially non-elliptic settings.

B.1.
Preliminaries

Throughout this section, we fix a closed densely defined linear operator between separable Hilbert spaces
\mathscr{H}_{1}\to\mathscr{H}_{2}
.
For reasons that will later become apparent we shall denote this operator by
T_{\rm min}
. We also fix an adjoint
T^{\dagger}_{\rm min}:\mathscr{H}_{2}\to\mathscr{H}_{1}
which is closed and densely defined. In the main part of this paper, we were exclusively concerned with
T_{\rm min}
being the minimal closure of an elliptic differential operator on a manifold with boundary and
T^{\dagger}_{\rm min}
the minimal closure of its formal adjoint. We introduce the notation

T_{\rm max}:=(T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})^{*}\quad\mbox{and}\quad T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}:=(T_{\rm min})^{*}.

Note that
T_{\rm min}\subseteq T_{\rm max}
and
T_{\rm min}^{\dagger}\subseteq T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}
.

Definition B.1
.

•

A pair
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
as above is said to be a formally adjointed pair (FAP).

•

A realisation
T
of a FAP is an extension
T
of
T_{\rm min}
contained in
T_{\rm max}
. If
T
is closed as an operator, we say that
T
is a closed realisation of
\mathcal{T}
.

•

The Cauchy data space of a FAP
\mathcal{T}
is defined as the Hilbert space

\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}:={\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}}.

The quotient map
\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})\to\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
will be denoted by
\gamma
.

The reader can note that also in this abstract setting, whenever
\check{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{T})
is a Banach space equipped with a surjective map
\gamma:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})\to\check{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{T})
with kernel
\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})
then
\check{\mathrm{H}}(\mathcal{T})\cong\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
via an isomorphism induced from the boundary mappings. We can therefore speak of concrete Cauchy data spaces also in this abstract setting.

If
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
is a FAP, then
\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}:=(T^{\dagger}_{\rm min},T_{\rm min})
is also a FAP. A symmetric closed operator
S
will, unless otherwise indicated, be identified with the FAP
(S,S)
. In particular,
S_{\rm min}=S=S_{\rm min}^{\dagger}
and
S_{\rm max}=S^{*}=S_{\rm max}^{\dagger}
. We use the notation
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{S}:=\check{\mathscr{H}}_{(S,S)}\equiv{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(S)}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(S)}$}}{\raisebox{2.67209pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(S)}$}}{\raisebox{1.8854pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(S)}$}}}
.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. Define the densely defined operator
\tilde{T}
on
\mathscr{H}\oplus\mathscr{H}
by

\tilde{T}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&T_{\rm min}\\
T_{\rm min}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}.

Then, the following hold.

(1)

The operator
\tilde{T}
is closed and symmetric, and its adjoint is given by

\tilde{T}^{*}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&T_{\rm max}\\
T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}&0\end{pmatrix}.

Both
\tilde{T}
and
\tilde{T}^{*}
are
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded in the grading of
\mathscr{H}\oplus\mathscr{H}
making the first summand even and the second odd.

(2)

The equality
\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{T}^{*})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max}^{\dagger})\oplus\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})
induces an equality

\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\tilde{T}}=\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}\oplus\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}.

(3)

The von Neumann decomposition of
\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{T}^{*})
induces an isomorphism of Hilbert spaces

\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}\oplus\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}\cong\ker(\tilde{T}^{*}+i)\oplus\ker(\tilde{T}^{*}-i).

We omit the proof as it follows from standard techniques for unbounded operators. The reader can find relevant details in
[
70
]
.

Lemma B.3
.

Let
S
be a closed symmetric operator.

(1)

The sesquilinear form

\omega_{S,0}:\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})\times\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})\to\mathbb{C},\quad\omega_{S,0}(v_{1},v_{2}):=\langle S^{*}v_{1},v_{2}\rangle_{\mathscr{H}}-\langle v_{1},S^{*}v_{2}\rangle_{\mathscr{H}},

descends to a well-defined sesquilinear non-degenerate pairing

\omega_{S}:\check{\mathscr{H}}_{S}\times\check{\mathscr{H}}_{S}\to\mathbb{C},

satisfying the symmetry condition

\omega_{S}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=-\overline{\omega_{S}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1})}.

(2)

Under the isomorphism
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{S}\cong\ker(S^{*}+i)\oplus\ker(S^{*}-i)
, the pairing
\omega_{S}
corresponds to the sesquilinear form

\displaystyle\omega_{S,vN}:

\displaystyle(\ker(S^{*}+i)\oplus\ker(S^{*}-i))\times(\ker(S^{*}+i)\oplus\ker(S^{*}-i))\to\mathbb{C},

\displaystyle\omega_{S,vN}\left(\begin{pmatrix}v_{1,+}\\
v_{1,-}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}v_{2,+}\\
v_{2,-}\end{pmatrix}\right):=2i\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}v_{1,+}\\
v_{1,-}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}v_{2,+}\\
v_{2,-}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{H}\oplus\mathscr{H}}.

Proof.

The analogous symmetry condition
\omega_{S,0}(v_{1},v_{2})=-\overline{\omega_{S,0}(v_{2},v_{1})}
for
\omega_{S,0}
is readily verified. To prove item
1
, we take
v^{\prime}\in\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})
and
v\in\mathrm{dom}(S)
and compute that

\omega_{S,0}(v,v^{\prime}):=\langle S^{*}v,v^{\prime}\rangle-\langle v,S^{*}v^{\prime}\rangle=\langle S^{*}v,v^{\prime}\rangle-\langle Sv,v^{\prime}\rangle=0,

since
v\in\mathrm{dom}(S)
and
S^{*}\supseteq S
. This computation, the symmetry condition on
\omega_{S,0}
and its sesquilinearity implies that
\omega_{S}
is a well-defined sesquilinear form satisfying the stated symmetry condition. To show that
\omega_{S}
is non-degenerate, if
v\in\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})
satisfies
\omega_{S}([v],\xi)=0
for all
\xi\in\check{\mathscr{H}}_{S}
then
\langle S^{*}v,v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathscr{H}}=\langle v,S^{*}v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathscr{H}}
for all
v^{\prime}\in\mathrm{dom}(S^{*})
so
v\in\mathrm{dom}(S^{**})=\mathrm{dom}(S)
so
[v]=0
in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{S}
.

Let us prove item
2
. We use the convention that the inner product is linear in the second leg. For
v_{1,\pm},v_{2,\pm}\in\ker(S^{*}\pm i)
,

\displaystyle\omega_{S}(v_{1,+}+v_{1,-},

\displaystyle v_{2,+}+v_{2,-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle S^{*}(v_{1,+}+v_{1,-}),v_{2,+}+v_{2,-}\rangle-\langle v_{1,+}+v_{1,-},S^{*}(v_{2,+}+v_{2,-})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle-iv_{1,+}+iv_{1,-},v_{2,+}+v_{2,-}\rangle-\langle v_{1,+}+v_{1,-},-iv_{2,+}+iv_{2,-}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2i\langle v_{1,+},v_{2,-}\rangle-2i\langle v_{1,-},v_{2,+}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{S,vN}\left(\begin{pmatrix}v_{1,+}\\
v_{1,-}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}v_{2,+}\\
v_{2,-}\end{pmatrix}\right).\qed

Proposition B.4
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. The sesquilinear form

\omega_{\mathcal{T},0}:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max}^{\dagger})\times\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})\to\mathbb{C},\quad\omega_{\mathcal{T},0}(v_{1},v_{2}):=\langle T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}v_{1},v_{2}\rangle-\langle v_{1},T_{\rm max}v_{2}\rangle,

descends to a well-defined sesquilinear non-degenerate pairing

\omega_{\mathcal{T}}:\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}\times\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}\to\mathbb{C},

satisfying the symmetry condition

\omega_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=-\overline{\omega_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}(\xi_{2},\xi_{1})}.

Proof.

That
\omega_{\mathcal{T}}
is well-defined and satisfies the symmetry condition follows from Proposition
B.2
and Lemma
B.3
. The proof that
\omega_{\mathcal{T}}
is non-degenerate goes along the same lines as in the proof of Lemma
B.3
and is omitted.
∎

B.2.
The Cauchy data space and boundary conditions

For a subspace
B\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{T}
, we define the realisation
T_{\rm B}
by

\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B}):=\{v\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max}):\gamma(v)\in B\}.

Proposition B.5
.

Let
(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. The construction
B\mapsto T_{\rm B}
sets up a one-to-one correspondence of (closed) realisations of
(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
and (closed) subspaces of
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{T}
.

Proof.

A short argument involving the five lemma shows that given a (closed) subspace
B\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
,
T_{\rm B}
is the unique (closed) realisation
T
with
\gamma\mathrm{dom}(T)=B
. A similar argument shows that given a (closed) realisation
T
, the subspace
B:=\gamma(\mathrm{dom}(T))\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
is the unique (closed) subspace
B_{0}
for which
T=T_{{\rm B}_{0}}
.
∎

Definition B.6
.

A subspace
B\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
is called a
generalised boundary condition
and a closed subspace
B\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{T}
a
boundary condition
.

If
B
is a generalised boundary condition, we define its adjoint boundary condition as

B^{*}:=\{w\in\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}:\omega_{\mathcal{T}}(w,v)=0\quad\forall v\in B\}.

In this terminology, Proposition
B.5
can be phrased as there being a one-to-one correspondence between closed realisations of a formally adjointed closed operator and its boundary conditions. The terminology adjoint boundary condition is motivated by the next proposition.

Proposition B.7
.

Let
(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP and
B
a generalised boundary condition. Then it holds that

(T_{\rm B})^{*}=T^{\dagger}_{{\rm B}^{*}}.

Proof.

We first show that
(T_{\rm B})^{*}\supseteq T^{\dagger}_{\rm B^{*}}
. If
v\in\mathrm{dom}T^{\dagger}_{\rm B^{*}}
then for any
w\in\mathrm{dom}T_{{\rm B}}
we have that

\langle T_{{\rm B}^{*}}^{\dagger}v,w\rangle-\langle v,T_{{\rm B}}w\rangle=\langle T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}v,w\rangle-\langle v,T_{\rm max}w\rangle=\omega_{\mathcal{T}}(\gamma(w),\gamma(v))=0.

Therefore,
v\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})^{*}
and
(T_{\rm B})^{*}v=T_{{\rm B}^{*}}^{\dagger}v
.

We prove
(T_{\rm B})^{*}\subseteq T^{\dagger}_{{\rm B}^{*}}
. As
T_{\rm B}\supseteq T_{\min}
,
(T_{\rm B})^{*}\subseteq T^{\dagger}_{\max}
so consider a
v\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max}^{\dagger})\setminus\mathrm{dom}(T^{\dagger}_{{\rm B}^{*}})
, i.e. a
v\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max}^{\dagger})
with
\gamma(v)\notin B^{*}
. Nondegeneracy of
\omega_{\mathcal{T}}
implies that there exists a
w\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}})
with
\omega_{\mathcal{T}}(\gamma(v),\gamma(w))\neq 0
, or equivalently that there exists a
w\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}})
with

\langle T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}v,w\rangle-\langle v,T_{\rm max}w\rangle=\langle(T_{\rm B})^{*}v,w\rangle-\langle v,T_{\rm B}w\rangle\neq 0.

This shows that
v\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})^{*}
so we conclude that
(T_{\rm B})^{*}\subseteq T^{\dagger}_{{\rm B}^{*}}
.
∎

Proposition B.8
.

Let
S
be a closed symmetric operator.

(1)

For any generalised boundary condition
B
,
S_{\rm B}^{*}=S_{{\rm B}^{*}}
.

(2)

A boundary condition
B
for
S
defines a self-adjoint realisation
S_{\rm B}
if and only if
B
is Lagrangian (i.e.
B^{*}=B
).

(3)

A boundary condition
B
for
S
defines a symmetric realisation
S_{\rm B}
if and only if
B
is isotropic (i.e.
B\subseteq B^{*}
).

Proof.

By definition,
S^{\dagger}_{\rm min}=S_{\rm min}=S
for the FAP constructed from a closed symmetric operator and therefore the proposition follows from Proposition
B.7
.
∎

Proposition B.9
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP and recall the notation from Proposition
B.2
.

(1)

There is a one-to-one correspondence between closed realisations of
\mathcal{T}
and self-adjoint
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded realisations of
\tilde{T}
given by

T_{\rm B}\longleftrightarrow\begin{pmatrix}0&T_{\rm B}\\
T^{\dagger}_{{\rm B}^{*}}&0\end{pmatrix}.

(2)

There is a one-to-one correspondence between closed subspaces
B\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded Lagrangian subspaces
\tilde{B}\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\tilde{T}}=\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{T}^{*})/\mathrm{dom}(\tilde{T})
given by

B\longleftrightarrow B^{*}\oplus B.

Proof.

By Proposition
B.7
and
B.8
, item
1
follows from item
2
. Let us prove item
2
. We note that

\omega_{\tilde{T}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}\\
v_{2}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}^{\prime}\\
v_{2}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\omega_{\mathcal{T}}(v_{1},v_{2}^{\prime})+\omega_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}(v_{2},v_{1}^{\prime}),

for
v_{1},v_{1}^{\prime}\in\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}
and
v_{2},v_{2}^{\prime}\in\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
. Therefore, given a
B\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
,
\tilde{B}:=B^{*}\oplus B
is a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded isotropic subspace (i.e.
\tilde{B}\subseteq\tilde{B}^{*}
). Given
(v_{1},v_{2})\in\tilde{B}^{*}
, the condition

\omega_{\tilde{T}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}\\
v_{2}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}^{\prime}\\
0\end{pmatrix}\right)=0,

for all
v_{1}^{\prime}\in B^{*}
implies that
v_{2}\in B
and the condition

\omega_{\tilde{T}}\left(\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}\\
v_{2}\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0\\
v_{2}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right)=0,

for all
v_{2}^{\prime}\in B
implies that
v_{1}\in B^{*}
which proves that
\tilde{B}^{*}\subseteq\tilde{B}
. We conclude that
\tilde{B}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded Lagrangian subspace. Conversely, given a
\mathbb{Z}/2
-graded Lagrangian subspaces
\tilde{B}\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\tilde{T}}
the fact that
\tilde{B}
is graded implies that
\tilde{B}=B^{\prime}\oplus B
for closed subspaces
B^{\prime}\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}\dagger}
and
B\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
and the fact that
\tilde{B}
is Lagrangian implies that
B^{\prime}=B^{*}
by an argument as above. We conclude that
B\leftrightarrow B^{*}\oplus B
produces a one-to-one correspondence as stated.
∎

Proposition B.10
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP and
B_{1}\subseteq B_{2}
be boundary conditions. Then
T_{{\rm B}_{1}}\subseteq T_{{\rm B}_{2}}
and there is a short exact sequence of Hilbert spaces

0\to\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}_{1}})\to\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}_{2}})\to{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}}\to 0.

In particular, the following special cases hold for a boundary condition
B
:

(1)

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})}$}}}\cong B
,

(2)

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B}$}}}
.

This follows from standard considerations with the open mapping theorem and the five lemma.

Definition B.11
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. We define the Hardy space as

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}:=\gamma\ker T_{\rm max}\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}.

It is readily verified that the Hardy space fits into a short exact sequence of vector spaces

0\to\ker(T_{\rm min})\to\ker(T_{\rm max})\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\to 0.

This also follows from the next proposition.

Proposition B.12
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP and
B
be a boundary condition. Then there is a short exact sequence of vector spaces

0\to\ker(T_{\rm min})\to\ker(T_{{\rm B}})\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\cap B\to 0.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
\ker(T_{\rm min})=\ker(T_{\rm max})\cap\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min})
and
\ker(T_{\rm B})=\ker(T_{\rm max})\cap\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})
.
∎

Let us briefly comment on the relation to boundary triplets. We compare our result to the setup of
[
35
]
.
For more details, history and context for boundary triplets, see also
[
23
,
10
]
.
We remark that if a FAP
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
admits a realisation
T
which is invertible, which is equivalent to
0\in\mathrm{res}(T)=\mathrm{res}(T^{*})^{\mathrm{conj}}
, then
T^{*}
is also invertible and therefore,
\ker T_{\rm min}=\ker T_{\rm min}^{\dagger}=0
.
Thus,
\gamma|:\ker T_{\rm max}\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
\gamma|:\ker T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}
are vector space isomorphisms.

Proposition B.13
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP admitting a realisation
T_{\beta}
which is invertible. Define
\mathrm{pr}_{\beta}:=T_{\beta}^{-1}T_{\rm max}:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})\to\mathrm{dom}(T_{\beta})
and
\mathrm{pr}_{\beta^{\dagger}}:=(T_{\beta}^{*})^{-1}T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max}^{\dagger})\to\mathrm{dom}(T_{\beta}^{*})
.
Then, the following hold.

(1)

It holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\beta})+\ker T_{\rm max},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max}^{\dagger})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\beta}^{*})+\ker T_{\rm max}^{\dagger},

and the mappings

\displaystyle\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{\beta}\oplus\gamma(1-\mathrm{pr}_{\beta})}\mathrm{dom}(T_{\beta})\oplus\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{\beta^{\dagger}}\oplus\gamma(1-\mathrm{pr}_{\beta^{\dagger}})}\mathrm{dom}(T_{\beta}^{*})\oplus\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}},

are isomorphisms.

(2)

The isomorphisms from
1
induce isomorphisms

\displaystyle\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}\cong\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T},\beta}\oplus\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}\cong\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger},\beta}\oplus\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}},

where
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T},\beta}:=\gamma\mathrm{dom}(T_{\beta})
and
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger},\beta}:=\gamma\mathrm{dom}T_{\beta}^{*}
are closed subspaces.

(3)

There is a one-to-one correspondence between boundary conditions
B
of
\mathcal{T}
and triples
(W,V,\mathfrak{T})
, where
V\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
and
W\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}
are closed subspaces and
\mathfrak{T}:V\dashrightarrow W
is a closed densely defined operator.

B.3.
Characterising Fredholm boundary conditions

In this section, we show how to characterise Fredholm boundary conditions for a FAP satisfying the extra condition that
T_{\rm min}
and
T_{\rm min}^{\dagger}
have closed range and
\ker(T_{\rm min})
and
\ker(T_{\rm min}^{\dagger})
being finite-dimensional. For the sake of terminology, we define this notion as follows.

Definition B.14
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. We say that
\mathcal{T}
has closed range if
T_{\rm min}
and
T_{\rm min}^{\dagger}
have closed range. We say that
\mathcal{T}
is a Fredholm FAP if it has closed range and
\ker(T_{\rm min})
and
\ker(T_{\rm min}^{\dagger})
are finite-dimensional.

The reader should note that by the closed range theorem,
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
is a FAP with closed range if and only if
T_{\rm min}
and
T_{\rm max}
have closed range. For the purpose of studying elliptic differential operators, the following sufficient condition for closed range and Fredholmness will be useful.

Proposition B.15
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. Assume that the inclusion
\iota:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{1}
is a compact inclusion. Then, the following holds.

(1)

\ker(T_{\rm min})
is finite-dimensional.

(2)

T_{\rm min}
and
T_{\rm max}^{\dagger}
have closed range.

(3)

\mathscr{H}_{1}=\ker(T_{\min})\oplus^{\perp}\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max}^{\dagger})
and
\mathscr{H}_{2}=\ker(T_{\max}^{\dagger})\oplus^{\perp}\mathrm{ran}(T_{\min})
.

In particular, if both
\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{1}
and
\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min}^{\dagger})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{2}
are compact inclusions, then
\mathcal{T}
is a Fredholm FAP and we additionally have that
\mathscr{H}_{2}=\ker(T_{\min}^{\dagger})\oplus^{\perp}\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})
.

Proof.

For
u\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm min})
, we have the estimate

\|u\|_{T_{\rm min}}\simeq\|\iota u\|_{\mathscr{H}_{1}}+\|T_{\rm min}u\|_{\mathscr{H}_{2}}.

Then, by Proposition A3 in
[
7
]
(ii), we conclude that
T_{\rm min}
has finite dimensional kernel and closed image.
The fact that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm min}^{*})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm max}^{\dagger})
is closed follows from the closed range theorem.
∎

Remark B.16
.

Note that, even without the assumption
\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min}^{\dagger})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{H}_{2}
is a compact embedding it still holds that
\mathscr{H}_{2}=\ker(T^{\dagger}_{\min})\oplus\overline{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}
.
The compactness of the embedding ensures that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})=\overline{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}
as well as the finite dimensionality of
\ker(T_{min}^{\dagger})
.

Lemma B.17
.

Let
K\subset Z
be a closed subspace of a Banach space
Z
and let
X\subset Z
a subspace.
If
K\cap X
is closed, the map
\Phi:{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}}\to{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{K}$}}}
defined by
\Phi[u]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}}}=[u]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{K}$}}}}
is well-defined and injective.

Proof.

To show the map is well-defined, suppose
[x]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}}}=[y]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}}}
.
Then,
x-y\in(K\cap X)\subset K
and therefore,
[x]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{K}$}}}}=[y]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{K}$}}}}
.

For injectivity, suppose that
\varphi[u]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}}}=0
, which is the same as
[u]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{K}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{Z}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{K}$}}}}=0
.
Then,
u=u-0\in K
and since
u\in X
, we obtain that
u\in K\cap X
.
That is,
u\in(K\cap X)
and therefore,
[u]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\displaystyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\textstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.67416pt}{$\scriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}{\raisebox{1.19583pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{X}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(K\cap X)}$}}}}=0
.
∎

Proposition B.18
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP and
B
a generalised boundary condition. The map

\Phi_{\rm B}:{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\to\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B}),\qquad\Phi_{\rm B}[u]:=T_{\max}u,

is a bounded bijection.
Moreover, the map

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\to{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}

given by

[u]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}}\mapsto[u]_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}}

is well-defined and is a bounded injection.

Proof.

To show that this map is bounded, we note that

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{\rm B}[u]\|

\displaystyle=\|T_{\max}u\|\leq\inf_{x\in\ker(T_{\rm B})}\|u-x\|+\|T_{\max}u\|

\displaystyle=\inf_{x\in\ker(T_{\rm B})}\|u-x\|+\|T_{{\rm B}}u\|=\|[u]\|_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.8375pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.3125pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}}.

Surjectivity is clear.
For injectivity, note that if
\Phi_{\rm B}[u]=0
, then
T_{\max}u=0
, so
u\in\ker(T_{\max})
.
But since
u\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})
, we have that
u\in\ker(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})\cap\ker(T_{max})
.

The map

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\to{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}

is a well-defined injection since
\ker(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})\cap\ker(T_{\max})
by invoking Lemma
B.17
.
∎

Lemma B.19
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP and
Y\subset\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})
a subspace. Consider the generalised boundary condition
B:=\gamma(Y)\subseteq\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
.
Then,
\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min})+Y
.

Proof.

Since
Y,\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min})\subset\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}})
, we have that
\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}})\supset\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min})+Y
.

To prove the opposite containment, let
u\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})
, which is if and only if
\gamma(u)\in B=\gamma(Y)
.
Therefore, there exists
y\in Y
such that
\gamma(u)=\gamma(y)
.
So,
u-y\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min})
and
y\in Y
so
u=(u-y)+y\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min})+Y
.
∎

Lemma B.20
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. For a generalised boundary condition
B
, we have that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})
and that
\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})+\ker(T_{\max})
.

Proof.

It is clear that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})\subset\mathrm{ran}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})
.
Set
Y=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})+\ker(T_{\max})
and apply Lemma
B.19
to obtain that
\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})+\ker(T_{\max})
, since
\mathrm{dom}(T_{\min})\subset\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})
.
Then, for
u\in\mathrm{ran}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})
, we can find
v\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})
such that
u=T_{\max}v
.
By what we have just proved, we have
v=x_{\rm B}+y\in\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})+\ker(T_{\max})
and therefore,
u=T_{\rm max}v=T_{\rm max}x_{{\rm B}}
.
I.e.,
u\in\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
.
∎

Lemma B.21
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP. For a generalised boundary condition
B^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\subset B^{\prime}
, we have that the inclusion map

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}

is an isometry.
If
\mathcal{T}
has closed range, the normed space
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
is closed if and only if
B^{\prime}
is a boundary condition, i.e., closed in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof.

Note that the induced norm on
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
is

\|[u]\|^{2}=\inf_{x\in\ker(T_{\max})}\|u-x\|^{2}+\|T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}}u\|^{2}=\inf_{x\in\ker(T_{\max})}\|u-x\|^{2}+\|T_{\max}u\|^{2},

where the norm after the ultimate equality is the norm on
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
.
If
B^{\prime}
is closed, then
\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})
is closed and therefore,
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
is a Banach space, i.e. it is closed in
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
.
On the other hand, if
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
is closed, then
\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})
is closed since
\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}^{\prime}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}\oplus\ker(T_{\max})
.
∎

Lemma B.22
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP with closed range. For a generalised boundary condition
B
,
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
is closed if and only if the image of the map
\Xi:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})\to{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
given by

\Xi:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})\to{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}

has closed range.

Proof.

We first start by noting that, by Proposition
B.18
, the open mapping theorem yields that the we have that the map
\Phi_{\max}
as defined in Proposition
B.18
is homeomorphism between
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
and
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})
.

Moreover, by the definition of
\Phi_{\rm B}
, we have the following commuting diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\rm B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{\max}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{2}}

Note that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})\subset\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})
, so
\iota_{2}
is simply the inclusion map.
Since
\Phi_{\max}
is a Banach space isomorphism, we have that

\Phi_{\max}^{-1}{{\lvert}}_{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}:\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})\to\iota_{1}\left({\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\right)

is bounded.
Therefore,
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
is closed if and only if
\iota_{1}\left({\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\right)
is closed.
The latter is, indeed, the image of
\Xi
so this proves the lemma.
∎

Theorem B.23
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP with closed range. For a generalised boundary condition
B
,
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
is closed if and only if
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is a boundary condition.

Proof.

Lemma
B.20
yields that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})
.
Then, Lemma
B.22
says that this is closed if and only if the range of the map
\Xi
is closed.
Since
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\subset B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
, it is easy to see that the range of
\Xi
is precisely
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
B.21
, we obtain that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
is closed if and only if
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is closed, i.e., that
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is a boundary condition.
∎

Corollary B.24
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP with closed range and
B
a generalised boundary condition. Then the Hardy space
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is closed in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof.

The corollary follows directly from Theorem
B.23
with
B=0
.
∎

Lemma B.25
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP with closed range. When
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
is closed,

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}}\oplus{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}\cong\ker(T_{\min}^{\dagger})\oplus{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}.

Proof.

By assumption
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})
is closed and therefore,

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}_{2}

\displaystyle\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}}\oplus\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}}}\oplus{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\oplus\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B}).

From Lemma
B.20
, we have that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})
and
T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}
is a closed operator from Lemma
B.21
.
Thus, note that
\ker(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})=\ker(T_{\max})
and therefore,

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\max})}$}}}={\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}}\cong\mathrm{ran}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B}).

Thus,

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{dom}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}

from Proposition
B.10
.
∎

Proposition B.26
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a Fredholm FAP. For a general boundary condition
B
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
has finite algebraic codimension in
\mathscr{H}_{2}
;

(2)

T_{\rm B}
has closed range and
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})^{\perp}
is finite dimensional;

(3)

T_{\rm B}
has closed range and
\ker(T_{\rm B}^{\ast})
is finite dimensional;

(4)

B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
has finite algebraic codimension in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
;

(5)

B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is closed and has finite algebraic codimension in in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

If any of these hold, then

\ker(T_{\rm B}^{\ast})\cong{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}{\raisebox{1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathscr{H}_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})}$}}}}\cong\ker(T_{\min}^{\dagger})\oplus{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}}.

Proof.

The equivalence of
1
-
3
,
as well as
4
-
5
are standard.
The statements
5
and
2
are equivalent because of Lemma
B.20
coupled with Lemma
B.25
.
The formula follows from the fact that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})^{\perp}\cong\ker(T_{\rm B}^{\ast})
along with Lemma
B.25
.
∎

Before proceeding with studying Fredholm properties of realisations, we make a remark about the maximal domain in terms of the Hardy space and a suitable realisation.

Corollary B.27
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a FAP with closed range and
B
be a boundary condition. Then
B
is a complement to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
if and only if
T_{\rm B}
has closed range and satisfies that

\ker(T_{\rm min})=\ker(T_{\rm B})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\ker(T_{\rm min}^{*})=\ker(T_{{\rm B}^{*}}^{\dagger}).

In this case,

\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})\oplus_{\ker T_{\rm min}}\ker(T_{\rm max}).

The reader should note that closed complements of the Hardy space in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
always exist for a FAP with closed range since
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
is a Hilbert space and the Hardy space is closed by Corollary
B.24
.

Proof.

Assume that
B
is a complement to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
. The operator
T_{\rm B}
has closed range by Theorem
B.23
. Let
P:\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
denote the projection along
B
. We then arrive at a short exact sequence

0\to\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})\to\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})\xrightarrow{P\circ\gamma}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\to 0.

(33)

In particular, we have the short exact sequence

0\to\ker(T_{\rm B})\to\ker(T_{\rm max})\xrightarrow{\gamma}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\to 0,

showing that
\ker(T_{\rm min})=\ker(T_{\rm B})
. Moreover, the short exact sequence (
33
) implies that the map
\Phi_{\rm B}
from Proposition
B.18
is a bijection, and so
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})
. Since
B
is a complement to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
,
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
