is closed, so
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{\max})
implies that
\ker(T_{\rm min}^{*})=\ker(T_{{\rm B}^{*}}^{\dagger})
.

Assume now that
T_{\rm B}
has closed range and satisfies that

\ker(T_{\rm min})=\ker(T_{\rm B})\quad\mbox{and}\quad\ker(T_{\rm min}^{*})=\ker(T_{{\rm B}^{*}}^{\dagger}).

The first equality ensures that
B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}=0
. The second equality and Proposition
B.26
implies that
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}=\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
. Therefore
B
is a complement to
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

The equality
\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm max})=\mathrm{dom}(T_{\rm B})\oplus_{\ker T_{\rm min}}\ker(T_{\rm max})
follows from Equation (
33
).
∎

Now, we characterise Fredholm boundary conditions via the language of Fredholm pairs.

Theorem B.28
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a Fredholm FAP and
B
be a boundary condition. The following are equivalent:

(1)

(B,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})
is a Fredholm pair in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
;

(2)

T_{\rm B}
is a Fredholm operator.

If either of these equivalent conditions hold, we have that

B^{\ast}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}.

Moreover,

\indx(T_{\rm B})=\indx(B,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})+\dim\ker(T_{\min})-\dim\ker(T_{\min}^{\dagger}).

Proof.

Since
B
is closed,
T_{\rm B}
is a closed operator and
\ker(T_{\rm B})
is a closed subspace of
\mathscr{H}_{1}
. If
1
holds, then
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is closed and by Theorem
B.23
, we have that
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})
is closed. Similarly, if
1
holds then
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
has finite codimension and
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})^{\perp}
has finite codimension in
\mathscr{H}_{2}
by Proposition
B.26
.
Moreover,
B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is finite dimensional, so Proposition
B.12
yields that
\ker(T_{\rm B})
is finite dimensional and therefore,
T_{\rm B}
is Fredholm.

On the other hand if
2
holds, i.e.,
T_{\rm B}
is Fredholm, then
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})=\mathrm{ran}(T_{{\rm B}+\mathcal{C}})
is closed, so Theorem
B.23
implies that
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is closed. Moreover, by Proposition
B.12
we have that
B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\ker(T_{\min})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\ker(T_{\min})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{\ker(T_{\min})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\rm B})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\ker(T_{\min})}$}}}
is finite dimensional.
Moreover
\mathrm{ran}(T_{\rm B})^{\perp}
has finite codimension and so by Proposition
B.26

5
,
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
has finite codimension in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
.
That is,
(B,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})
is a Fredholm pair in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
.

From a similar calculation, we obtain that

\ker(T_{\rm B}^{\ast})=\ker(T^{\dagger}_{{\rm B}^{\ast}})\cong\ker(T_{\min}^{\dagger})\oplus(B^{\ast}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}}).

By the formula in Proposition
B.26
, we obtain the desired formula.
Plugging this into the index, we obtain

\displaystyle\indx(T_{\rm B})

\displaystyle=\dim\ker(T_{\rm B})-\dim\ker(T_{\rm B}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\dim\ker(T_{\min})+\dim(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})-\dim\ker(T_{\min}^{\dagger})-\dim(B^{\ast}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}^{\dagger}})

\displaystyle=\indx(B,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})+\dim\ker(T_{\min})-\dim\ker(T_{\min}^{\dagger}).\qed

We say that a boundary condition of a FAP is a Fredholm boundary condition if the associated realisation is a Fredholm operator.

Corollary B.29
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a Fredholm FAP. Let
B_{1}\subseteq B_{2}
be generalised boundary conditions with
B_{1}
being a Fredholm boundary condition. Then,
B_{2}
is a Fredholm boundary condition if and only if
B_{2}/B_{1}
is finite dimensional. In this case, the following index formula holds:

\indx(T_{{\rm B}_{2}})=\indx(T_{{\rm B}_{1}})+\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}}.

Proof.

Assume that
B_{1}
has finite codimension in
B_{2}
, so clearly
B_{2}
will be closed. It is easy to see that
B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is finite dimensional and since
B_{1}\subset B_{2}
, we have that

\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}\leq\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}.

That is,
(B_{2},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})
is a Fredholm pair and therefore, we have that
B_{2}
is a Fredholm boundary condition.

Assume now that
B_{2}
is a Fredholm boundary condition, i.e. that
B_{2}
is closed and
(B_{2},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})
is a Fredholm pair. Note that

\displaystyle\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle\cong(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})\oplus{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}

\displaystyle\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}\oplus(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})\oplus{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}

and therefore,

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}\oplus{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}}\oplus{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}.

Since
(B_{2},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})
and
(B_{1},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})
are Fredholm pairs,
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}
and
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}
are finite-dimensional and we conclude that
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}}
is finite dimensional.

For the index formula, we note that it suffices to prove that

\indx(B_{2},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})=\indx(B_{1},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})+\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{2}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B_{1}}$}}}.

This identity follows from the computation

\displaystyle\indx(B_{2},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})

\displaystyle=\dim(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})-\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}

\displaystyle=\dim(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})+\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}+\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}{\raisebox{4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.76909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.76909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}}

\displaystyle=\indx(B_{1},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})+\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.75pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.625pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}{\raisebox{1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})}$}}}+\dim{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}{\raisebox{4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.76909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{2}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.76909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B_{1}}}$}}}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

In addition to Theorem
B.28
, a useful way to obtain Fredholm boundary conditions is given by the following Proposition which follows from
[
7
, Proposition A.1]
.
It is adapted from
[
7
, Corollary 8.8]
, although
[
7
]
only considered the context of regular boundary conditions. The reader can, in the context of elliptic boundary value problems, compare the construction of the next proposition to standard constructions in the Boutet de Monvel calculus
[
16
,
60
,
43
]
. Again, the provenance of the next result dates back further than
[
7
]
and can be found in for instance
[
43
, Lemma 4.3.1]
.

Proposition B.30
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a Fredholm FAP with a boundary condition
B
. Choose a closed complementary subspace
\check{C}
of
B
in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
with projection
\check{P}:\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}\to\check{C}
defined along
B
(for instance the orthogonal complement).
The map

\check{L}_{\check{P}}:\mathrm{dom}(T_{\max})\to\begin{matrix}\mathscr{H}_{2}\\
\oplus\\
\check{C}\end{matrix},\quad\check{L}_{\check{P}}u=\begin{pmatrix}T_{\max}u\\
\check{P}\gamma u\end{pmatrix},

satisfies:

(1)

T_{\rm B}
has closed range if and only if
\check{L}_{\check{P}}
has closed range.

(2)

The range of
T_{\rm B}
has finite codimension if and only if the range of
\check{L}_{\check{P}}
has finite codimension, in which case

\dim\mathrm{coker}(T_{\rm B})=\dim\mathrm{coker}(\check{L}_{\check{P}}).

(3)

\ker T_{\rm B}
has finite dimension if and only if
\ker\check{L}_{\check{P}}
has finite dimension, in which case

\dim\ker(T_{\rm B})=\dim\ker(\check{L}_{\check{P}}).

(4)

\check{L}_{\check{P}}
is Fredholm if and only if
T_{\rm B}
is Fredholm.

We also note the following: Fredholm boundary conditions must necessarily be infinite dimensional.

Proposition B.31
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(T_{\rm min},T^{\dagger}_{\rm min})
be a Fredholm FAP such that
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
has infinite codimension in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
. If
B
is a Fredholm boundary condition then
\dim B=\dim B^{*}=\infty
.

Proof.

We prove via the contrapositive.
Let
B\subset\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
be a finite dimensional subspace.
Indeed, this is a boundary condition and
B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is closed since
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
is closed.
Now,

\displaystyle\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}

\displaystyle B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=B\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}=(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B}\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}.

From this, we obtain

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.51909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.85738pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}}

and

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B}\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B}\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B}\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.51909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.85738pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B}\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}}.

Therefore,

{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}\oplus(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.51445pt}{$\displaystyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-4.26445pt}{$\textstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.2589pt}{$\scriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}{\raisebox{2.51909pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.85738pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}}$}}}\cong{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}.

Since
(B\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}})^{\perp,B}
is finite dimensional and
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}
has infinite codimension in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
, we have that
{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\displaystyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\textstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}{\raisebox{3.14236pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{B+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}
is infinite dimensional.
That is,
B
is not Fredholm. By the contrapositive, if
B
is Fredholm, then
\dim B=\infty
. Since
B^{\ast}
is Fredholm if and only if
B
is Fredholm, a symmetric argument yields that
\dim B^{\ast}=\infty
.
∎

B.4.
Rigidity results for the index

Let us prove a homotopy invariance result for the index of Fredholm realisations of Fredholm FAPs.

Theorem B.32
.

Assume that
\mathcal{T}_{t}=(T_{t,{\rm min}},T^{\dagger}_{t,{\rm min}})_{t\in[0,1]}
is a family of Fredholm FAPs
\mathscr{H}_{1}\to\mathscr{H}_{2}
such that

(i)

The mappings

[0,1]\ni t\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}0&T_{t,{\rm min}}\\
T^{\dagger}_{t,{\rm max}}&0\end{pmatrix}\quad\mbox{and}\quad[0,1]\ni t\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}0&T^{\dagger}_{t,{\rm min}}\\
T_{t,{\rm max}}&0\end{pmatrix}

are continuous in the gap topology on the space of densely defined self-adjoint operators (i.e. the resolvents are norm continuous with respect to
t
).

ii)

\mathrm{dom}(T_{t,{\rm min}})
and
\mathrm{dom}(T_{t,{\rm max}})
are constant in
t
, so
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}:=\mathrm{dom}(T_{t,{\rm max}})/\mathrm{dom}(T_{t,{\rm min}})
is independent of
t
up to a canonical Banach space isomorphism.

Let
[0,1]\ni t\mapsto P_{t}\in\mathbb{B}(\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}})
be a norm continuous family of projectors such that
B_{t}:=(1-P_{t})\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies that
(B_{t},\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T}_{t}})
is a Fredholm pair in
\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Then it holds that

\indx(T_{0,B_{0}})=\indx(T_{1,B_{1}}).

The proof uses standard techniques from
KK
-theory and the index theory of Fredholm operators on Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules. For an overview, see
[
54
]
and
[
32
]
.

Proof.

We write
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T}}
for the Hilbert space
\mathrm{dom}(T_{t_{0},{\rm max}})
for some fixed
t_{0}
and note that
T_{t,{\rm max}}:\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T}}\to\mathscr{H}_{2}
is continuous for each
t
. By assumption i) above, the family
(T_{t,{\rm max}})_{t\in[0,1]}
defines an adjointable
C[0,1]
-linear map that we denote by
T_{{\rm max}}:\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T},C([0,1])}\to\mathscr{H}_{2,C([0,1])}
where
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T},C([0,1])}
and
\mathscr{H}_{2,C([0,1])}
denote the
C[0,1]
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-module defined from the indicated Hilbert spaces. We also set
\check{C}_{t}:=P_{t}\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
and let
\check{C}_{C([0,1])}
denote the
C[0,1]
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-submodule of
C([0,1])\otimes\check{\mathscr{H}}_{\mathcal{T}}
with fibre
\check{C}_{t}
, it is well-defined by norm continuity of the family
(P_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
.

Following Proposition
B.30
, we define the operator

\check{L}_{t}:\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T}}\to\begin{matrix}\mathscr{H}_{2}\\
\oplus\\
\check{C}_{t}\end{matrix},\quad\check{L}_{t}u=\begin{pmatrix}T_{t,\max}u\\
P_{t}\gamma u\end{pmatrix},

for
t\in[0,1]
. This operator defines an adjointable
C[0,1]
-linear map
\check{L}:\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T},C([0,1])}\to\mathscr{H}_{2,C([0,1])}\oplus\check{C}_{C([0,1])}
because
T_{{\rm max}}:\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T},C([0,1])}\to\mathscr{H}_{2,C([0,1])}
and
\xi\mapsto(t\mapsto P_{t}\xi)
is an adjointable map
\check{\mathrm{H}}(D)_{C([0,1])}\to\check{C}_{C([0,1])}
.

We claim that the adjointable
C[0,1]
-linear map
\check{L}:\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T},C([0,1])}\to\mathscr{H}_{2,C([0,1])}\oplus\check{C}
is Fredholm. For each
t\in[0,1]
,
\check{L}_{t}
is Fredholm, so we can find finite-dimensional vector bundles
E_{t,1},E_{t,2}\to[0,1]
with
E_{t,1}
being a subbundle of
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T},C([0,1])}
such that
E_{t,1}|_{t}=\ker\check{L_{t}}
and
E_{t,2}
being a subbundle of
\mathscr{H}_{2,C([0,1])}
such that the induced map
E_{t,2}|_{t}\to\mathrm{coker}\check{L_{t}}
is surjective. In particular, there is a finite-rank perturbation of
\check{L}
as a mapping
\mathscr{H}_{\mathcal{T},C([0,1])}\oplus E_{t,2}\to\mathscr{H}_{2,C([0,1])}\oplus\check{C}\oplus E_{t,1}
which is invertible when restricted to the fibre over
t
. By an openness argument, this finite-rank perturbation of
\check{L}
is invertible on a small open neighbourhood
U_{t}
of
t
. In particular,
\check{L}
is Fredholm when restricted to
U_{t}
. By compactness of
[0,1]
,
\check{L}
is Fredholm.

Since,
\check{L}
is a Fredholm operator between
C[0,1]
-Hilbert
C^{*}
-modules, it has a well-defined index in
K^{0}([0,1])
. Under the isomorphisms
K^{0}([0,1])\cong K^{0}(pt)\cong\mathbb{Z}
, homotopy invariance of
K
-theory implies that

\indx(\check{L}_{0})=r_{0}^{*}\indx_{C([0,1])}(\check{L})=r_{1}^{*}\indx_{C([0,1])}(\check{L})=\indx(\check{L}_{1}),

where
r_{t}
denotes the inclusion
\{t\}\subseteq[0,1]
. We can now conclude the theorem from Proposition
B.30
which implies that

\indx(T_{0,B_{0}})=\indx(\check{L}_{0})=\indx(\check{L}_{1})=\indx(T_{1,B_{1}}).\qed
